**Remote throughput policy (user request, 2026-09-21):** maximize useful remote parallelism,
not nested oversubscription. Probe CPU affinity/count, available RAM, active work and free disk
before a batch; budget the sum of worker compiler/model threads against that capacity. The current
server reports 12 logical CPUs and about 115 GiB available RAM: use up to 12 aggregate compiler
jobs (for existing -j4 leaf runners, three concurrent isolated runs). Keep unique run tags and
source/output directories; multi-script `remote py --threads N` now isolates each script's outputs.
The protected model remains at its pinned single simulation thread; changing compiled thread count
requires a separate build identity and qualification. Run independent leaf/formal/checker tasks
alongside it where budgets permit. Serialize shared-tree mutation and experiments explicitly testing
host-load effects. Never kill another live workload to make room: routine run/soak/di/shell preflight
now refuses overlap without automatic pkill. Retain incomplete/error results; no retry-to-green.

##### S0 RESULT (executed): the PMP stop is a compiler-control artifact, not session RTL

Single-axis experiment, two builds from ONE unmodified worktree source state, differing only in the
pinned `split-counter.vlt` bound via `SOFT_LADDER_BUILD_VLT_ARGS`; serial soaks on frozen
`fw_payload.elf` `6b2bad99...`; control hash preflight-verified `be176b27...`:

| Arm | Model | Control | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `s0-recipe-ctl-v1` | `480ceed1...` | present | `SUCCESS (tohost=0) after 12765628 cycles`; hart0=333635, hart1=8932406 |
| `s0-recipe-noctl-v1` | `70da7cac...` | absent | `pmp_entry.sv:81` at cycle 12731487 |

Prediction confirmed; rejection condition (both arms failing identically) did not occur. Arm ctl
matches the historical anchor cycle count and both retirement counts exactly. Conclusions:
- The recurring 12,731,487 stop belongs to the omitted qualified lzc `split_var` control.
- Every attribution derived from the earlier recipe-mismatched builds is void, including the
  frontend-source bisect and the claim that non-source inputs were identical.
- Nothing was weakened: no PMP logic, assertion, vendor file or waiver changed; control stays opt-in.

Two load-bearing qualifications, both recorded rather than smoothed over:
1. **No runner-certified verdict existed for the original two runs.** The optional
   `SOURCE_REVIEW_REFERENCE_TRACE` / `EXTEND_REFERENCE` check raised after the simulation but before
   `outcome`/`strictDualPassed` were computed, so both arms exited rc=1. Verdicts for those runs are
   raw `trial/run.log` lines with the three strict stores confirmed in-trace. Strong evidence, not a
   certified anchor pass; certified observations were obtained later (below).
2. **Non-regression, not evidence for the fetch repair.** Matching cycle/retirement counts does not
   measure kill-persistence activation. Its positive qualification remains open under S1.

S0 ATTRIBUTION ESTABLISHED, AND ONE RETRACTION WITHDRAWN. The 8-run repeatability matrix (serial,
idle builder, runner pinned `ad4561bf...`, references unset) gives ctl 5/5 `outcome: pass` +
`strictDualPassed: true` at 12,765,628 cycles with identical 333,635/8,932,406 retirements, and noctl
3/3 `outcome: error` rc=255 at `pmp_entry.sv:81` @ 12,731,487. Zero overlap on one varied axis, so the
compiler-control attribution now rests on repeats, and the protected anchor is runner-CERTIFIED for
the ctl arm.

An intermediate pass claimed arm ctl "never passed, it hung", on the grounds that
`*** SUCCESS *** (tohost = 0)` after a `[trapdump]`/`[fdtmem]`/`[walk]`/`[hangpc]` dump must be a
hang-exit. That is WRONG and is withdrawn: those markers and that banner appear in all five certified
passes — they are unconditional end-of-sim dumps and this harness prints `tohost = 0` on success. The
original in-trace strict-store evidence was right. Calibrate a diagnostic marker on a known-good run
before overturning a result with it. What survives: read verdicts from the runner's
`outcome`/`strictDualPassed`, never from simulator stdout.

**Early termination remains unowned; do not report a flake probability.** Retained run
`s0-recipe-ctl-certified-v1` was incomplete at 1,531,692 cycles. The later `s0-cert-ctl-r6`
was incomplete at 3,573,269 cycles, with 325,956/2,109,370 retirements and no tracer-termination
marker. Its 4,870,657 trace lines match the passing replay prefix exactly. This is early
termination, not demonstrated architectural divergence. Model/firmware hashes and log prefixes do
not prove identity of all external inputs or process-control events. Neither a population failure
rate nor intrinsic simulator nondeterminism is established by these observations.

Source review found a concrete process-control hazard: proxy run/soak/di/shell preflight invoked a
global harness pkill; even a shell query merely mentioning the binary name took that path.
`g6lc_tb.cpp` handles SIGTERM by calling `dtm->stop()`, and its banner prints the exit code rather
than raw tohost memory. The automatic kill calls have been removed with regression tests, while
overlap refusal is retained. This supplies a plausible external-stop mechanism, not proof of the
sender or cause in either historical run. Keep both incomplete results as nonpassing evidence.

Matching historical cycle/retirement counts is a non-regression observation; it does not measure
whether kill-persistence logic activated. Positive recovery evidence still requires S1's independent
observer and collision cases. No activation count was established by the anchor comparison.

Reference divergence now RESOLVED by decode, no extra simulation: pc `0x80008662` holds
`0x1607b52f` — AMO opcode `0101111`, funct5 `00010` (LR), funct3 `011`, rs2=x0, rd=x10 =
`lr.d.aqrl x10,(x15)`. An LR performs no store, so the reference's two-value `mem 0x80046088 0x0`
is a phantom write; current traces omit it because `cva6_rvfi.sv` clears `lsu_wmask` for
`AMO_LRW`/`AMO_LRD`. It lands on the first LR retirement, exactly as that repair's comment predicts.
The hypothesis is confirmed with a mechanism, and the retained `opensbi-amo-ready-dual-20260919`
reference is stale and architecturally wrong. Re-baselining is justified on that recorded ground
only; keep the prefix check in the flow, move its baseline, record the new reference hash, and never
certify a model against its own trace. S0 also gains a follow-on: make a missing/mismatched compiler control a
preflight refusal and record generated command/dependency inputs in the build manifest, so recipe
identity is comparable without a soak.

##### Findings that changed the next work (pre-experiment analysis, retained)

1. **The anchor comparisons omitted a real compiler input.** New read-only proxy inspections
   `stability-reassessment-work-ver-smt2-loadcancel-v2-recipe-v1` and
   `stability-reassessment-work-ver-smt2-truezero-v1-recipe-v1` captured the generated build
   command from `Variane_testharness__verFiles.dat`. The passing `f35d0e10...` includes
   `/opt/testharness/runs/pmp-transition-split-20260919/output/source/split-counter.vlt`;
   failing `145c8d74...` does not. Both report the same corrected runtime and firmware hash.
   `AGENTS.md` already documents this lzc scheduling control; section A below records its
   qualified SHA256 `be176b279ada076a3459d8bd6509e0946ccf0994d5c35a092bede308bba8c8ff`.
   The 689-source hash comparison did not cover this external build input. Stop the
   allowlist-string/translate_off elimination loop: these were not matched build recipes.
   Missing control is the leading explanation of the recurrent PMP stop, not a fresh boot PASS.
   The current candidate must still be rebuilt and booted with the complete pinned recipe.
2. **PMP line81 checks decoder consistency, not whether firmware supplied a malformed pair.**
   `size = trail_ones + 3`; the first non-trailing-one bit must be zero for a settled binary
   input. A different pmpaddr still defines a trailing-one count. Inspect lzc evaluation and
   sampled consistency before changing PMP RTL or its assertions. No PMP weakening is approved.
3. **Do not restore the reverted load assertion patch as-is.** For the RV64 three-bit offset,
   `(offset[1:0]==0) -> offset<5` and `(offset[0]==0) -> offset<7` are tautologies; exhaustive
   enumeration of offsets0..7 confirms no failing case. Retaining `$fatal` did not retain
   detection strength. Current load_unit has the original antecedents and plain fatal text.
   Replace the premature-check contract with precise exception/kill/completion obligations
   and an independent bad-completion mutation before any instrumentation change is accepted.
4. **Fetch cancellation is a transaction-lifetime problem, not an address-filter proof.**
   Current kill_owed state clears on a replacement request, compares only VA, and samples
   one inflight address although READ permits response/new-request overlap. Same-address
   refetch, response concurrent with kill, multiple kills, FDIP and hart/context changes are
   not qualified. The probe now observes DUT kill_owed state and therefore cannot independently
   catch loss of that state. Keep the frozen before/after as local evidence, not proof of the
   whole repair. Prove an ordered single-outstanding contract or carry the necessary identity;
   do not invent a generation width without proving its reuse bound.
5. **Several earlier deductions are withdrawn as general rules.** Repeatable failures do not
   exclude deterministic compiler/scheduling faults. Synthesis translate_off intentionally
   leaves simulation assertions active. A wired flush is not proof of end-to-end cancellation.
   Address equality is not transaction identity. A failure with OoOEn=0 shows OoO is not
   necessary for that symptom; it does not exonerate every backend interaction. In this
   FTQ-enabled integer profile, SRC_EX sets arch_step=FtqEn and npc_d may be next_block(mtvec):
   npc=0x80000218 does not establish arch_valid=0 for mtvec=0x80000210. Cache hits can return
   in one cycle, so 3-vs-8 cycles alone cannot prove stale supply. Stage34 also changes handler
   content/alignment/layout, not solely a branch target; TvalEn=1 in g6lc64_ooo_int means the
   unconditional zero-mtval allowance is too weak for that profile.

##### Method transferred from the historical frontend work

Use contracts and ownership, not the old predicates: firmware is a witness, not a controller;
recovery membership follows owner/age, not value/PC plausibility; prediction is not resolution;
a hint cannot waive architectural state changes. `fetch_A`/`smt_legacy` stay excluded from actual
source graphs; `core/smt` holds live shared support. Historical A/B advice transfers as a
matched baseline/candidate experiment on fetch_B, not permission to resurrect the retired RTL.
The runtime-learning guide qualifies the older heuristics: a consequence is not causal proof,
absence needs a working observer, repetition is not independent evidence, and every stored
lesson needs scope and invalidation conditions. Do not count repeated narrative revisions as
progress. Retain one independently checked artifact per contract family.

##### Coherent large passes, with attributable internal changes

A large pass owns an end-to-end contract and its tests, runners, proof and documentation together.
It is not an omnibus rewrite. Keep independent mechanisms separable and gate each locally before
one integrated qualification sweep. No feature promotion, issue-width reduction, firmware/DTS
workaround, new clock/reset, or change of physical target is part of this review.

**S0 — Reproducible qualification foundation (first).**
- Change surface: `soft-ladder-build-harness.sh`, proxy build manifests, source/FP review runners,
  their host tests and target-specific verify selection. Preserve protected/experimental guards.
- Record and verify actual generated commands/dependencies, expanded flists, overlays/defines,
  effective config, external compiler-control contents/hashes, tool/runtime/header identities,
  generated bootrom, model, ELF/DTB, seed and completion contract. A source-map hash is not enough.
- Reuse `run_pmp_transition_review.py` positive/reference-negative/decoder-mutation and its
  independent match proof; compare the established split_var recipe without editing vendor PMP
  or disabling assertions. Missing/wrong control must be a preflight refusal for the qualified
  profile, not an opt-in check silently omitted by the caller. Do not change global tool policy.
- Exit: refusal controls pass, the known anchor recipe is reproducible, then the current SMT2
  candidate completes the strict dual-hart contract with that recipe. Keep historical failures
  as mismatched-recipe evidence. No further full-source bisect until recipe identity is settled.

**S1 — Precise recovery across frontend, I$ and LSU (stability-critical).**
- Change surface: fetch_B frontend/pkg/realigner/queue, `g6lc_icache`, load_unit/LSU, existing
  frontend/LSU leaf fixtures, formal properties and the stage32-34 runner/testlist surface.
- Map offer, accepted request, translation, response, kill, new epoch and consumed redirect
  instruction. Include demand, prefetch and loop-buffer suppliers; arch flush and predicted
  branch kill are not interchangeable. Prefer cancellation at the owning cache/request state
  when that closes the contract; retain a frontend filter only if independently justified.
- Check same-cycle request/response/kill, killed response after replacement, same-address refetch,
  back-to-back kills, hits/misses/translation waits/errors, reset, hart switch, FTQ on/off and
  RVC split targets. Include replay in the same lifecycle review instead of adding a blind flush
  term; replay is already suppressed by arch_valid and needs its own collision observation.
- Replace the reverted tautological assertion idea with transaction-associated precise
  misalignment checks: correct cause/PC/tval for the configured envelope, no destination update,
  no forbidden MMIO effect, and no architectural trap from cancelled work. Test byte/half/word/
  doubleword offsets and fault/older-response coincidence. Keep malformed normal-completion
  detection fatal and prove a mutation can trip it. Make mtval expectations config-aware.
- Independent observer/reference must not derive cancellation truth from kill_drop/kill_owed.
  Keep the original frozen failing ELF and observer non-interference pair. Implement source-
  reproducible layout variants with controlled link/layout and verified disassembly; do not
  claim one-axis control from two generally re-linked binaries.
- Exit: live-boundary safety/cover and restored-defect controls, preserved aligned-load and
  redirect paths, frozen failing case repaired, protected in-order control and targeted structural
  checks. Then retire temporary G6LC_NO_KILL_PERSIST infrastructure after retaining mutation tests.

**S2 — Complete backend ownership and memory-effect lifetimes.**
- Change surface: scoreboard, dispatch/rename/PRF/IQ/ROB/LSQ/memdep, IRO, load/store/bypass/AMO
  units, commit and RVFI. Preserve the existing pre-grant cancellation repair and its controls.
- Build one lifecycle inventory for every producer: allocation, queue residence, FU acceptance,
  completion, usable data, architectural commit, cancellation, release and ID reuse. Prove that
  tombstones/quarantine cover all old responders, or carry generation/owner metadata where
  suppression cannot be proved. Include divider/multiplier/FPU and pending stores, not only LSU.
- Keep exception completion distinct from usable data, physical reclamation from completion, LR
  retirement from reservation lifetime, and commit-time CSR/AMO data from early placeholders.
  Cover paired retirements, older-live/younger-cancelled work, resource saturation and wraparound.
- Close memory-order interactions: byte-overlap forwarding from youngest older stores, pending
  committed stores across recovery/handoff, MMIO authorization, fences and constrained LR/SC.
  Unconstrained SC differences remain explicitly self-checking; do not suppress other mismatches.
- Exit: live-port/bounded or inductive ownership checks with reachable covers and mutations,
  independently checked single-hart integer/FP execution, exact retirement/memory effects,
  and no latches/SCCs in actual enabled full-core cones. STA remains a separate requirement.

**S3 — Finish two-hart coarse-handoff architectural stability.**
- Change surface: cva6 integration, core/smt banks/scheduler, rename owner/committed state, LSU/MMU,
  CSR/trap/interrupt plumbing, FPR/frm/fflags and RVFI/PMU attribution. Reuse existing structures.
- Preserve quiesce plus issued/store drain. Prove no outgoing operation observes an incoming
  hart's privilege/SATP/ASID/PMP context, and no peer trap/flush destroys surviving owned work.
  Test both hart roles, duplicate architectural destinations and VAs, WFI/IPI/HSM stop/start,
  exceptions with pending traffic, LR/SC/AMO/fence publication and FP context restoration.
- Use per-hart ordered reference comparison; shared memory needs an allowed-outcome/happens-
  before oracle, not one rigid Spike global interleave. Add negatives for wrong hart, stale
  context, lost wakeup, peer squash and cross-class result/flag corruption.
- Exit: both harts perform checked work under the declared drained-handoff envelope, including
  trap/interrupt/FP controls; protected in-order remains intact. SMT/FP guards stay until each
  proposed supported configuration independently clears its promotion gate.

**S4 — Integrated SMT2+OoO firmware and release-evidence pass.**
- Freeze one final source/recipe tuple after S0-S3. Run named integer/FP/dual-hart suites and
  negatives, protected in-order OpenSBI/HSM, then experimental OoO dual-hart firmware with
  explicit qualificationOnly/substitution provenance. Bounded progress or a banner is not PASS.
- Run target-specific lint/elaboration, relevant proofs and covers, live-port leaf and enabled
  whole-core synthesis. Default cv32a65x/cv64a6 smoke is not OoO/SMT2 structural qualification.
  Strict standalone slang unavailable means that gate is open; no allow-skips promotion.
- Report source/recipe/model/workload hashes, completion predicates, expected SC freedoms,
  assertion/refusal controls, observer equivalence, failures and missing gates together.
- Physical exit remains separate: source-bound STA at the inferred 1.25GHz target and declared
  corners with approved libraries/macros, plus DFT/CDC and power/activity evidence. Record
  fetch-accept comparator fanout, IQ age scans and PRF port cost; do not claim timing from lint.
- Update spec-to-impl/tests, derived coverage, design docs, architecture and this plan from one
  evidence matrix. Promotion of guards/defaults is a later explicit decision, not a boot side effect.

**S5 — Mixed residency, only after coarse stability; not folded into S0-S4.**
Design per-hart retirement/recovery/translation context and shared-resource ownership before
removing drain. Only then evaluate elastic IQ/PRF/LSQ credits, biased fetch, FU fallback or other
policy changes. Acceptance requires simultaneous live work from both harts, same-hart ordered
retirement, peer recovery isolation and useful-work/solo comparisons. Drained handoff cannot
establish the latency-overlap or throughput claims of true mixed-resident SMT.

Dependency order: S0 before costly requalification; S1 and S2 are independently reviewable
correctness sets; S3 integrates their contracts; S4 freezes and qualifies the combined result;
S5 is a separate architecture gate. FPGA/RVH and conditional FP widths are explicit matrix cells,
not inferred from RV64 integer cases. CASQ, PMU residuals, predictor/checkpoint arbitration,
coherence/inclusive notifications, hierarchy concurrency, deferred snoop precision, Linux,
compliance/liveness and joint performance/physical qualification remain open in the later phases.

This pass performs source review, read-only metadata capture and documentation reconciliation
only. It neither applies the above RTL changes nor declares the current worktree qualified.

#### Historical investigation: exception redirect and offset assertion (2026-09-20)

Re-read the coding philosophy §0/§2.9/§2.10, the reasoning-pattern workflow (P1-P7,
T1-T10, ladder L0-L7), the heuristics (H1-H7 incl. H3 precondition and H6 veto), the SMT2
logics inference chain, NEGATIVE.md and this plan. Applied H3 first: validate the oracle.

The `addr_offset0` abort is an INSTRUMENT defect. A misaligned load is legal; the LSU
raises cause=4 and kills the request, but the D$ request is granted a cycle before the
exception is known, so the offset legitimately enters the load buffer. Directed stage32
aborts at796 cycles on OoO and1582 in-order, so it was never an OoO signal. `$fatal` and
its contradicting comment are both upstream (`git show HEAD`). Antecedents are now
qualified by the captured offset's alignment; no exception, kill or memory rule relaxed,
`translate_off` only. Firmware witness: va=0x2f offset=7 tid=0 commit_tid=4 — younger than
the commit head; `speculative=0` is uninformative at SpeculativeSb=0; the small-non-zero
address inference is refused per five recorded reverts.

Repairing the oracle exposed a REAL, still-open finding. Two frozen ELFs of the same
source differing only in branch immediates, same model f0344d76..., each replayed twice
deterministically: `phase-v1` traps to mtvec 0x80000210 with mcause=4/mepc=0x800001f8 and
mrets to 0x800001fc (PASS907); `after-v2` resumes at 0x80000206 — the sequential PC after
the faulting instruction's co-issued group — never entering the handler (FAIL tohost=1).
The promise violated is commit→frontend redirect. Mechanism NOT identified; no RTL change
made on it. H4 is satisfied (deterministic per ELF), so this is layout/geometry sensitive,
consistent with P2 product-not-factor.

PARTITION ANSWERED — NOT an OoO defect. `isolated-config-overlay.py` now allowlists
`OoOEn` (line-anchored so it cannot also match `SliceOoOEn`, which its uniqueness check
caught), giving a one-axis control from ONE source state: `OoOEn:1->0` on g6lc64_ooo_int,
single hart, two issue ports, model e02f1f2d... Replaying the same two frozen ELFs:
after-v2 -> OoO FAIL (0x80000206) and in-order TIMEOUT (cause=4 then no retirement);
phase-v1 -> OoO PASS907, in-order PASS925. It reproduces at OoOEn=0, so the two-issue OoO
path is exonerated; the symptom differs by backend (in-order stops retiring, OoO restarts
wrong), consistent with a shared trap-restart defect. Do not fix this inside the OoO
increment or count it against it. The in-order arm reprinted the cap banner and was
correctly classified `timeout`.

Restart source OBSERVED with +smt_flow_trace (no rebuild): alloc id=15 pc=0x800001f8 and
id=16 pc=0x800001fc in one 2-issue group at cycle781; wb id=15 ex=1 at785; commit-dbg
cause=4 at t=798; then alloc id=0 gen=11 pc=0x80000206 at789 and it retires at792.
0x800001fe/0x80000202 are never allocated, so the frontend resumed the SEQUENTIAL stream
instead of the commit-supplied mtvec 0x80000210, although controller.sv clears
set_pc_commit_o on ex_valid_i exactly so PC-gen takes the trap vector. Owner: PC-gen
priority between the commit exception PC and a frontend-internal redirect; controller.sv
already flags this seam for "early load-misalign/illegal".

Candidate co-factor REFUTED. The prediction was that the failing ELF's two consumer branches
sharing one target was the co-factor. Stage34 reproduces the geometry byte for byte
(8-aligned faulting load, 2-byte co-issued op, straddling 4-byte branch) WITH a shared
target, and PASSES on both the OoO and in-order control models. It is not vacuous: the trace
shows LD_ADDR_MISALIGNED @0x800001f8 followed by 0x80000210, the correct mtvec. Per T3 the
hypothesis is replaced, not softened.

Residual, currently unexplained: the same shape fails with the shared target 0x8000025e and
passes at 0x8000027e, although both are 2-byte aligned and sit at offset6 of their 8-byte
window. No supported candidate remains, so improve the observation rather than patch. Do NOT
reorder PC-gen redirect priority — NEGATIVE.md records TAGE RAS restore -> IAF
mepc=0x1400000000, reseed -> PC=0x4 and the ~1,600-predicate g1* soup in exactly that area.
Stage34 is retained as a passing regression (ooo_load_misaligned_shared_target) so this
geometry is now guarded by a seconds-long directed test instead of a firmware soak.

MECHANISM IDENTIFIED by improving the observation (still no repair). The exception flush is
CORRECT in both arms: failing cycle=786 flush=1 flush_unissued=1; passing cycle=789 identical.
The divergence is the frontend's next supply — failing allocates gen=11 pc=0x80000206 only 3
cycles after the flush; passing allocates gen=11 pc=0x80000210 (mtvec) after 8. Eight cycles is
a genuine redirect plus I$ access; three is too few for a refetch, so the failing arm delivers
supply that was ALREADY BUFFERED at the flush. The cycle=791 mispredict to 0x8000025e is a
consequence, not the fault, and the tohost=1 verdict is downstream of that.

FIRST RECLASSIFICATION, THEN WITHDRAWN (T9, same session). I recorded "P3 channel3 — fetch-side
state survived the kill, owned by the fetch_B supply queue/FTQ" on the strength of the latency
gap, before checking the structure. instr_queue.sv refutes that specific mechanism: flush_i is
wired to both FIFO instances and the same condition resets idx_ds_q/idx_is_q/push_seq_q, so the
instruction queue IS cleared on flush. The channel-3 attribution is withdrawn to a hypothesis
and no owner is named.

Facts that stand: the commit-side exception flush is correct in both arms; the failing arm's
first post-flush allocation is a PRE-FLUSH-STREAM PC (0x80000206, sequential after the faulting
instruction) rather than mtvec; it arrives in 3 cycles against 8 for the correct redirect, so the
arms differ in the PROVENANCE of the delivered instruction, not only in a computed address; the
cycle=791 mispredict to 0x8000025e and the tohost=1 verdict are downstream of that.

INSPECTION DONE. Every frontend structure holding a fetched instruction or PC across flush_if is
flush-qualified: instr_queue (.flush_i), realigner leftover (.flush_i + leftover_kill), FTQ and
loop buffer (flush_i | is_mispredict | bp_fire), predictor (flush_i | is_mispredict | flush_bp_i),
icache_valid_q (flush_i || is_mispredict || bp_fire), inflight_q (kill_s2). The ONLY
non-flush-qualified state is replay_q/replay_addr_q (frontend.sv:170-178), which also outranks
the sequential and prediction cases in npc_select — a genuine latent asymmetry, logged
separately. It is NOT this defect: +fetch_win_trace shows rp=0 at the exception flush.

DECISIVE NEGATIVE: the [win] line at the exception flush is IDENTICAL in both arms —
vaddr=0x80000208 k1=1 k2=1 bpf=0 fl=1 mp=0 rp=0 npc=0x80000218 (failing t=799, passing t=802).
arch_valid is low in BOTH (npc took the if_ready branch), so "the trap vector was not selected at
the flush cycle" is equally true of the passing arm and explains nothing.

THREE candidates eliminated, none weakened: shared branch targets; a queue entry surviving the
kill; replay_q survival / arch_valid-at-flush.

DIVERGENCE ISOLATED — a killed I$ response is accepted one cycle after the flush:
  passing  t=802 kill 0x80000208 (k1=1 k2=1 take=0 fl=1) -> t=808 take=1 vaddr=0x80000210 (mtvec)
  failing  t=799 kill 0x80000208 (k1=1 k2=1 take=0 fl=1) -> t=800 take=1 vaddr=0x80000208
           bpf=1 vmask=0111 una=1 npc=0x80000248 -> t=804 fl=1 mp=1 -> t=812 0x8000025e (fail)
The failing arm accepts the response for the very window it killed, pushes three instructions and
fires a prediction from it, which moves npc off the trap vector; the mispredict and the fail exit
are consequences. Promise violated at the I$ RESPONSE-ACCEPTANCE boundary: kill_s1/kill_s2 are
single-cycle, inflight_q is cleared by kill_s2 in that cycle, and icache_take is no longer
inhibited once fl=0, so a response landing the next cycle is taken. Channel3 again but at the
response boundary, not the queue (whose flush qualification was verified).

CONFIRMED by a dedicated instrument. +fetch_kill_check (frontend.sv, translate_off, report-only;
allowlisted in corev_apu/tb/g6lc_tb.cpp — an unlisted plusarg goes to HTIF and kills the run before
the probe arms, which is how the first attempt failed) tracks "an outstanding fetch was killed and
not replaced", so a real redirect clears it and only a stale take can report:
  failing  [killchk] t=800 take=1 rsp_vaddr=80000208 killed_vaddr=80000208 same=1 npc=80000248
  passing  silent for the whole run
One report, failing arm only, at exactly the divergence cycle, same=1 proving the accepted response
is the killed address. The passing arm never takes a killed response in 2000 cycles, so this is not
a benign coincidence. Chain closed by observation: killed response accepted -> trap vector never
fetched -> mispredict into the fail exit.

REPAIR IMPLEMENTED — kill persistence in frontend.sv. kill_owed_q/kill_owed_addr_q remember a killed
in-flight fetch until its response is seen or a replacement fetch is issued, and icache_take is gated
by kill_drop, which drops a response only when its ADDRESS matches the killed one. The address compare
is what keeps a legitimate return from being dropped, and it is an address match rather than a
decision from fetched data, so visibility channel5 stays closed. No new clock or reset, always_ff /
always_comb separation preserved, build clean at baseline 9 warnings with none added.

Before/after on the matched frozen pair, same model:
  after-v2   FAIL -> PASS 906; probe reports take=0 drop=1 same=1 once at t=800, npc=0x80000218
  phase-v1   PASS 907 -> PASS 907, probe silent
Regression: stages 17,20,22,25,29,30,31,32,33,34 Spike-matched
(918/913/936/959/896/894/906/906/927/902); stage 26 self-checking PASS 911. Stage 26 must NOT be

BLOCKER — THE REPAIR REGRESSES THE PROTECTED ANCHOR. Rebuilt in-order SMT2 model
3a1e97422efbc8c3..., same firmware and profile as the anchor: the boot reached 12,731,487 cycles

CONTROL RUN — THE REPAIR IS EXONERATED. A G6LC_NO_KILL_PERSIST seam disables only the drop (state and

  repair ON   3a1e97422efbc8c3...  pmp_entry.sv:81 at 12,731,487
  repair OFF  64fcafecf9aabc73...  pmp_entry.sv:81 at 12,731,487
Same assertion instance, same cycle. The repair neither causes nor influences the failure, and the

ALL THREE DELTAS ARE PROVABLY INERT, which moves suspicion from the source to the build. frontend.sv

writing that new owner's result at118644, before its real byte arrives at118647.
The adjacent old load also writes the replacement branch TID. The instruction
access fault is downstream of this demonstrated identity violation.

The old late-TID "unreachable stimulus" conclusion omitted **ungranted** requests
waiting in lsu_bypass. Post-grant load-buffer tombstones do not cover them. Repair:

#### OoO memory ordering repaired (2026-09-20)

Applying the §0 weigh-then-discriminate cycle and the RT-P1/RT-P2 instrument and
identity rules, I wrote three directed tests for contracts rather than for symptoms,
and each discriminates: OoO fails, in-order passes the identical frozen ELF, Spike
agrees with in-order. Before-traces are identity-tagged, so the mechanism is
observed rather than inferred — store_commit id=8 (0x333) preceding id=7 (0x222),
and load tid7 writing back data forwarded from store tid8.

Two contracts were violated in `core/store_buffer.sv`, both artefacts of the
in-order assumption that arrival order is program order:
 (A) hazard/forward matched on address only, so a younger store could supply an
     older load; commit-queue entries must stay exempt from the age test because
     their tids may be recycled;
 (B) the speculative queue was a FIFO in issue order, so same-address stores
     reached memory reversed. Fixed by program-order insertion, restoring the
     head-is-oldest invariant the rest of the module already assumed.
The hazard age filter is a liveness requirement: stage19 HUNG before the repair,
and the harness printed "SUCCESS" while doing so — outcome/timedOut in results.json
is the only trustworthy verdict.

Results: stages17/18/19 FAIL929 / FAIL949 / TIMEOUT -> PASS915 / 940 / 952, all
Spike-matched; negatives fail 928/943/938; leaf fixture 32 records; regressions
878/974/883/1057/1109 and FP 1471/1251/988 unchanged; guarded dual-hart 664 /
586 / 582 / 475 unchanged; protected in-order SMT2 boot re-run at exactly
12,765,628 cycles and 333,635/8,932,406; 44 host tests pass; lint 10 vs baseline 11.
Full-core synthesis for the earlier increment passed clean (2 warnings); a fresh
run for this increment is in flight and must not borrow that result.

Self-critique tests of the repair itself: stage20 (older store with late DATA must
still be waited for — the over-filtering failure mode) PASS911, and stage21 (five
younger stores reordered ahead of a delayed oldest) PASS930 with the overtaking
confirmed in the trace, so neither passes vacuously.

**Third defect found and repaired.** Stage22 raises
the load to ten outstanding stores against `DEPTH_SPEC=8` and deadlocks: younger
stores fill every slot, the oldest can never post, and the queue drains only on
in-order commit. The previous model times out identically, so it is not a
regression; the in-order control passes at1792. A deeper queue cannot fix a
liveness bug, and gating in the store buffer or store unit would re-deadlock on the
one-deep store pipe, so admission had to move to issue. Chose **program-order store
issue** in `g6lc_iq.sv` over IQ capacity reservation: no new cross-module signal on
the recently repaired issue path, and stores cannot retire out of order anyway, so
the ILP cost should be small. It is an admission rule over UNISSUED entries, which
is why it avoids the live-store-gate deadlock the file already warns about.

Measured: stage22 TIMEOUT -> PASS933 Spike-matched; stages17-21 915/940/952/911/931;
S4/S5/S15/memdep/ILP all identical at 878/974/883/1057/1109 — no measurable ILP
cost; leaf 32 records; prove+cover pass; FP 1471/1251/988; dual-hart 664/586/582/475.
The IQ lives inside `if (CVA6Cfg.OoOEn)` in issue_stage.sv, so in-order is
structurally unaffected. The store_buffer program-order insertion is now defence in
depth rather than the sole guarantee, retained as the assertion's subject and
flagged as removable if STA dislikes the insertion crossbar.

**AMO ordering probed; apparent fourth defect retracted as an instrument fault.**
At `amoadd.d` the trace logged the pre-operation memory value while `rd` was right
and the program's read-back saw the added value — a contradiction that indicts the
observation, per RT-P1. A second `amoadd.d rd, x0, (addr)` (reads memory at commit,
cannot be forwarded) returned 0x807, as did a following load, so memory and AMO
ordering are sound. The real fault is in `cva6_rvfi.sv`: rs1/rs2 are latched at
DISPATCH keyed by `issue_pointer`, while `rvfi_rs*_o` are the ISSUE-time operands
keyed by issue port — the RT-P2 failure of joining channels by port index and cycle
proximity. Under OoO those are different instructions, so AMO `mem_wdata` is
computed from a foreign rs2. Consequence: AMO memory effects are silently unchecked
by trace comparison — a blind spot in the method used throughout, now recorded. It
does not retract earlier results (other fields; no AMOs in those programs), and the
comparator was deliberately NOT masked. Stages23/24 are self-checking instead.

**RVFI operand capture repaired.** `issue_read_operands` exports
`rvfi_operand_valid_o`/`rvfi_operand_tid_o` (the issuing entry's trans_id beside the
operands it actually reads), routed through `issue_stage`, `cva6.sv` and
`rvfi_probes_instr_t`; `cva6_rvfi` re-captures rs1/rs2 against that identity under
`OoOEn`, leaving the in-order path untouched. AMO memory effects are now checked
for the first time: stages23/24 qualify against Spike, and all13 directed and
regression programs still match with unchanged cycle counts on model
`6180bbc8e219402c…`. The probe struct changes for every config, so byte-identity
no longer covers in-order; the protected boot was re-run and reproduces
strictDualPassed at 12,765,628 cycles and 333,635/8,932,406.

**LR/SC against a speculative store is sound (stage25, PASS956)** and exposed a
second instrument fault on the same principle: `lsu_wmask` was keyed on
`fu == STORE`, but every AMO takes that path and load-reserved only reads, so the
tracer emitted a memory write the architecture never performs. Probe now carries
`lsu_ctrl_op` and excludes AMO_LRW/LRD. All fourteen directed and regression
programs then match Spike with unchanged cycle counts on `3d1a304213e29328…`.
A failed SC also writes nothing and has no directed test yet — named, not assumed.

**Fifth defect found, pre-existing, and REPAIRED.** Chasing the failed-SC case, a
plain store that FOLLOWS a load-reserved never retires under OoO. Stage26 (LR +
store + SC) times out; stage27 aims the store at an unrelated address and still
times out, so it is neither reservation logic nor same-address ordering. In-order
passes both (1751/1736). The frozen ELF times out on all four model generations
including the pre-change baseline, so this session did not cause it. The existing
dual-hart LR/SC tests pass because they place `sc.d` immediately after `lr.d` with
no intervening plain store. Refined symptom: the store is allocated, becomes the
commit head (commit_ptr=3), never writes back, and ~24 instructions allocate behind
it before dispatch stalls with the ROB not full; no store_offer ever appears.

Attribution took two corrections, each forced by an observation rather than by
argument, which is the intended §0 cycle actually working:
 1. Proposed the depth-1 amo_buffer never releasing.
 2. WEAKENED it by reasoning `retire => ack => pop`. That was an inference, and a
    store-unit probe refuted it: `st_ready=0 sb_rdy=1 amo_rdy=0`, buffer occupied.
 3. Then the extended probe refuted the original hypothesis too: `amo_ack=1` at
    cycle800, the buffer drains, `st_ready` recovers, the store unit goes idle and
    `valid_i` never rises. The store unit is measured and CLEARED; the store is
    withheld upstream and never reaches the LSU.
Stage28 (no fence) hangs identically, so flush/re-push is not required. 26/27/28
all hang on all four model generations including the pre-change baseline, so this
session's store-admission rule is not implicated.

Root cause then read off the issue path and confirmed by the pre-existing
`issue_stall` diagnostic: `lrsc=1` is the only asserted blocker while every other
term is ready. `lr_sc_pair_q` blocks non-SC STORE issue to protect the AXI
exclusive reservation for OpenSBI cmpxchg, and closes only on SC issue or flush.
In-order always gets one of those while the LR is live; OoO need not, and an LR
with no SC is legal RISC-V. Repaired under OoOEn by remembering the LR's trans_id
and closing the window once that LR is no longer live (`fwd_i.still_issued`,
i.e. `issued & ~cancelled`) — no new port, in-order untouched, and it closes
exactly when no younger SC can still complete the reservation.

Stages26/27/28 now complete at 896/880/873 instead of timing out; everything else
unchanged and Spike-matched. Stage26 stays self-checking: with a same-hart store
between LR and SC the sequence is unconstrained, so CVA6 failing the SC and Spike
succeeding are both legal. It also caught the third RVFI write-mask instance — a
FAILED SC was reporting a memory write — now suppressed at commit.

**Firmware on dual-hart OoO is now measurable, and the first measurement FAILS.**
Added an explicit experimental-model mode to `run_opensbi_source_review.py` instead
of renaming a field to slip past its identity guard. The hash binding stays
mandatory in every mode; only the field name `modelSha256` is now accepted beside
`executableSha256`. Two new refusals keep the modes honest (substituted model
without the flag; flag without a substituted model), and the default refusal was
verified before use. Results carry `experimentalModel`, `protectedAnchor: false`,
`modelQualificationOnly`, `modelHarts` and the substitution list, so an
experimental boot can never be mistaken for the protected anchor.

Result: `strictDualPassed: false`, hitting the 16M cycle cap, hart0 9,805,054 vs
hart1 42,592 — inverted against the anchor's 333,635/8,932,406 — with
`mcause1=0x1` (instruction access fault) and `wfi1=1`. The banner prints SUCCESS
after 16000000 cycles, which is the cap; the record is authoritative. No firmware
claim is made. Next thread: hart1's fault PC and the faulting fetch under OoO.

**Sixth defect found AND repaired — and it unblocked two-hart mutual exclusion.**
The stall traced to `flu_ready=0` with a full scoreboard and both harts still in
the barrier, never reaching the lock. Root cause read from the code: `csr_buffer`
is depth-1 and hart-agnostic and holds `csr_ready` low from issue until COMMIT;
`ex_stage` computes `flu_ready = csr_ready & mult_ready`, which IRO turns into
"all FLU units busy" for both harts. In order a CSR is effectively oldest when
issued, so it commits promptly; out of order it can be stranded behind older FLU
consumers while younger work fills the scoreboard. Repaired in `g6lc_iq.sv` by
issuing a CSR only when it is the oldest live instruction — restoring the buffer's
own depth-1 assumption rather than weakening it, and reusing the commit anchor the
IQ already has. Same shape as the store-admission and LR/SC-window repairs.

**The two-hart AMO lock test now passes on OoO at 5,883 cycles (1,600/1,952),
faster than the in-order control's 7,392.** Cost: a uniform +3 cycles across the
directed/regression set. All 19 programs Spike-matched; dual-hart startup/LR-SC,
the FP suite and the protected boot (12,765,628 / 333,635 / 8,932,406) unchanged.

**Original finding, now superseded:** With the repairs in
place I ran the existing SMT_LOCK mode (both harts contending for one amoswap
lock) on the guarded dual-hart OoO model for the first time: it times out at 200k
cycles with 35/39 retirements — both harts stalled, not spinning. In-order passes
the same ELF in 7,392 cycles with 1,600/2,037 retirements. Stage29 (amoswap then
store, the critical-section shape) passes single-hart at 893 and matches Spike, so
the store-after-AMO path is exonerated; the LR/SC window is LR-only so it is not
involved either. Mechanism unidentified, no owner claimed.

This is the honest bound on "SMT2+OoO works": reset rendezvous and LR/SC consumer
sequences pass, but two-hart mutual exclusion does not, and spin locks are a
prerequisite for OpenSBI/Linux. No firmware-level dual-hart OoO claim is made.

Pattern worth retaining: three times now, a first-look "core defect" in an
AMO/LR-bearing program was actually the observation. The program's own
self-check and an unforwardable read settled each one faster than trace analysis.

Still open: cross-hart memory ownership, failed-SC trace case,
late-TID reuse, traps/interrupts, FP across harts, wider misalignment, and STA for
the added store-path comparators. No guard relaxed, no default target changed.
