#### B. Completion and evaluation rules

Track each mechanism as proposed -> reproduced -> leaf-qualified -> integrated ->
architecturally checked -> performance-characterized -> promotion-eligible. Store
source/model/tool/config/workload identities, assumptions, assertion/coverage scope,
positive and negative verdicts, cost, remaining gaps and invalidated older evidence.
An ERROR, timeout, skipped check or compiler-only success cannot advance a gate.

Use the SMT2 reasoning trio and coding/SoC philosophy: oracle validity and repeatability
before attribution; first violated interface promise before repair; one semantic
change per candidate; no firmware-address/register-specific conditions. Ordered
retirement comparison is per logical hart, including values, privilege, traps and
memory effects. Global interleave may legally differ; duplicate collapse, unordered
multisets and empty trace digests are not equivalence. Shared-memory outcomes need
an independent happens-before/RVWMO oracle, not one rigid cross-hart interleave.

Baseline balancing applies ONLY while both workers are enabled, have unfinished work
and are runnable under the declared environment. Parked WFI, stopped HSM harts,
serial coldboot and legal unbounded external backpressure are separate categories.
Do not call their unequal retirement counts scheduler starvation or saturation.

Measure, per hart and per ROI: runnable/active/service cycles, fetch and issue
acceptance, useful retirements, issue/LSU/queue stalls, miss/refill latency,
requested/pending/completed handoffs, drain/refetch cost, trap/flush events and
longest eligible wait. Separate no-work, blocked-external, blocked-shared-resource,
and denied-service time. Trace owner/TID/allocation-generation across boundaries.
Observers must be read-only, bounded, off/on equivalent and fault-sensitive.

Saturation requires independently checked work from BOTH workers, not only a
shared cookie or high occupancy. Use synchronized fixed-work ROIs: identical
compute/branch kernels; unequal compute versus pointer-chase; dependent and
independent loads; two streaming sets below/above each cache; shared-line ping-pong;
and wake/stop/resume phases. Preserve RVI/RVC, hart-role swap and worker-count controls.
Report single-worker baselines, per-hart slowdown, normalized progress, weighted
speedup sum(IPC_shared/IPC_solo), worst-hart slowdown and aggregate useful throughput.
For identical continuously runnable workers assess balanced service under the
scheduler's actual contract, not exact cycle equality. State response/hold bounds
before asserting a finite wait bound; quantum/starvation knobs alone do not bound
an outstanding memory drain. Keep fairness and throughput separate verdicts.

#### B1. Intra-core resource arbitration and minimal software hints

This refinement governs Phases1/4/6/8/9. SMT siblings are separate architectural
harts/logical Linux CPUs sharing ONE physical execution budget. Do not equalize
runnable thread counts, raw IPC, or instructions between them as an optimization
objective. A 50/50 selected-cycle split is only a symmetry control for equal work.
Scheduling across physical cores is distinct from resource arbitration among their
siblings; SMT harts are not extra L1/coherence clients or independent cache capacity.

The objective is checked useful work per physical core (and measured area/energy),
subject to minimum forward service and declared tail-latency/QoS limits. Compare a
paired workload with the SAME work run alone on each SAME hart, in both role orders.
Report core batch makespan/throughput, each role's slowdown, common two-worker window,
and single-worker tail separately. Removing a busy-wait reporter is a harness repair,
not a hardware scheduling gain. Include a no-work sibling, a dependency-limited
worker, cache conflicts, lock holder/waiter and interrupt work; idle sibling capacity
must be available to useful eligible work without chasing equal retirement counts.

**Measured ordering constraint (2026-09-19) — read before scheduling any mechanism
below.** Three paired measurements now bound what arbitration can achieve on the
CURRENT drained handoff:

- symmetric compute (`smt2-balance-ordered`): batch ratio0.902
- asymmetric compute/memory (`smt2-core-capacity`): 0.929/0.926
- dependency-limited dep/ind (`smt2-dep-ceiling-v5`): **0.8985 in both role orders**,
  despite solo IPC of0.62 vs0.82 proving real issue slack exists

The canonical SMT win case is the WORST of the three. Instruction-level overlap is
identically zero because only one hart issues at a time, so the mechanisms that
redistribute front-end or issue-queue share among simultaneously resident harts have
NO headroom to capture. Attribution supports this: all83 handoffs fired on
anti-starvation, none on quantum, so the quantum knob is already inert.

Therefore, **Biased fetch, RS/IQ partitioning, leftover front-end slots and FU issue
fallback are all gated behind the Phase4 mixed-resident subgate** and must not be
implemented or tuned before it. Their expected gain on the present design is zero or
negative, and any improvement measured without mixed residency would be a measurement
artifact. The single exception is the yield/pause hint: the lock experiment
(`smt2-lock-holder-v2`) shows a spinning waiter costs the holder 2.3456x while
producing nothing, which is recoverable by admission control alone, with no change to
residency. Sequence the work as: yield/pause hint (pre-Phase4) -> Phase4 mixed
residency -> then, and only then, the redistribution mechanisms and QoS rate control.

Execution-path assessment and incremental change sets:

| Mechanism | Current seam / restriction | Required implementation and gate |
|---|---|---|
| Yield/pause hint (only pre-Phase4 item) | id_stage now recovers the PAUSE encoding 0x0100000F, which the decoder folds into a NOP, and pulses a per-hart hint into g6lc_thread_select | **IMPLEMENTED and qualified 2026-09-19** (model ba1f8f6a...). Sticky per-hart yield request, cleared on that hart's next activation, hands the core to an UNPAUSED ready peer. Ranked below anti-starvation so it cannot deny the service floor; requires an unpaused peer so two yielding harts fall through to the normal policy instead of ping-ponging; advisory only, never gating readiness; keys on the architectural hint alone, never on spin patterns, lock addresses or kernel PCs. Gated by existing ZihintpauseEn && NrHarts>1, no new config field. Gate met: on identical source but for the PAUSE word, the holder section falls 4462 -> 2837 cycles (2.1618x -> 1.3719x) with mutual exclusion still proven. No-op control: pre-change model d3b95654 on the same source reproduces 4462/2148/2064/2066 exactly. Boot preserved at the identical 12,765,628 cycles; lint/synth unchanged on all three targets; 38 tooling tests. Residual 1.37x is the starvation floor by design, not tuned. WFI-based yield and any Linux/firmware use remain unimplemented. |
| Biased fetch / admission | g6lc_thread_select -> cva6 active hart -> fetch_B -> id_stage; cva6 masks all issue admission during quiesce/halt | **Phase4-gated (was Phase6a).** Measured zero headroom today: dep/ind pairing returns 0.8985 and every handoff already fires on anti-starvation, so weighting or quantum changes redistribute slices that cannot overlap. After Phase4: registered per-hart preference/credits over legal candidates at safe boundaries, with an age/service floor and static fallback. Charge accepted slots, not repeated requests. Weight is a resource preference, not a task-count target. |
| RS/IQ partitioning | g6lc_iq has a shared compacting oldest-ready queue; g6lc_ooo_dispatch admits a whole group only if rename/ROB/IQ/LSQ fit; OoO+SMT currently refused | Phase4 then6b: owner-qualified occupancy, per-hart minimum reservations plus an elastic shared pool; bounds sum to actual capacity. Borrow unused free slots, never revoke live entries. Refund once on issue/cancel/flush, qualify allocation generations, preserve committed PRF state and per-hart retirement. Compare static partitions before elastic policy. |
| Leftover front-end slots | Current fetch_B selects a single hart; drained handoff forbids concurrent peer admission | Phase4 mixed-resident subgate then6c: initially opportunistic whole-group scheduling, not mixing lanes. Later partial/mixed groups need hart-tagged PCs/RVC fragments/predictor snapshots, per-hart prefix acknowledgments, atomic rename/ROB/LSQ reservation, cancellation and precise faults. Spare lanes are not available just because another hart exists. |
| FU issue opportunities | g6lc_iq stops selecting when an older ready candidate is not acknowledged; issue_read_operands resolves FU/RAW hazards | **Phase4-gated.** Treat capability-aware fallback as a separate tested change: candidate selection must know legal FU ports without creating ack->select combinational loops, speculative wakeups, duplicate issue or weakened store-age ordering. |
| QoS rate control | No qualified per-hart policy ABI or intra-core rate controller | Phase6d/8: budget NEW demand/prefetch admission in explicit units (accepted slots or bytes), bounded refill/burst and minimum progress. Work-conserving soft caps lend unused quota; deliberate hard caps may idle capacity and must report why. Never throttle responses, older stores, fences, invalidations or interrupt completion. |

Do not widen queues or duplicate banks before observing the constrained resource.
Budget extra occupancy/credit/age bits, port muxing and policy fanout; update policy
outside wake/select's critical combinational cone. Latency hiding requires genuine
independent work and mixed-resident correctness, not a higher queue-depth parameter.
Kernel locks can make a low-IPC hart the producer of a peer's progress: low IPC or a
long cache RTT is not by itself a reason to throttle it. Simultaneous SMT work is not
inherently faster than solo work; retain that option in software placement decisions.

Minimal software-facing contract, proposed and NOT yet advertised:

1. Keep the existing DT cpu-map thread0/thread1 under core0. Validate runtime Linux
   sibling masks, cache-sharing maps and scheduler domains, then evaluate placement
   across physical cores before pairing siblings where appropriate to workload and
   power policy. Never report two full independent core capacities or assume adding
   a sibling doubles throughput. No kernel-specific PC/register/address heuristics.
2. Start with read-only aggregated observations: per-core elapsed time and accepted
   issue/fetch slots; per-hart eligible-but-unselected time, accepted useful service,
   and at most one coarse shared-resource pressure event. Derive ratios in software
   over a declared window; do not add divider logic, instantaneous queue addresses,
   detailed operand ownership, or one counter per station. Selected cycles include
   stalls/drains and are NOT accepted work or utilization. Sticky miss/block bits
   are not exact lost-cycle attribution; separate quiesce time and not-ready state.
3. MEASURED (smt2-pmu-ownership-20260919): mhpmevent3 is SHARED between the harts.
   With a handshaked order, hart1 reads back hart0's selector1 and hart0 reads back
   hart1's selector2; a banked design would show0/0, and that hypothesis arm fails as
   required. mscratch is correctly per-hart (0x100/0x101), so the bank itself works and
   the result is not a fixture artifact. Source agrees: g6lc_smt_csr_bank instantiates
   one csr_regfile per hart but muxes perf_addr/perf_data/perf_we by active_hart_i into
   a SINGLE core-level perf_counters, and broadcasts scountovf/lcofi back to every bank.
   Consequences to treat as gates, not details: one hart's selector write clobbers the
   peer's; counts mix both harts because the event sources are core-wide commit/cache
   signals; any HPM CSR write suppresses counting for ALL counters that cycle (`we_i`);
   Sscofpmf MINH/SINH/UINH filtering applies the ACTIVE hart's privilege to a shared
   counter; and an overflow raises LCOFI in both harts, where either can clear OF.
   RISC-V defines mhpmcounterN/mhpmeventN as per-hart CSRs, so this is a conformance
   gap for NrHarts>1, not merely a missing feature. It must be closed before any PMU
   or fairness hint is advertised. mcycle/minstret ARE banked; minstret is gated by
   commit_instr_i.hart_id, but mcycle advances in every bank each cycle, so mcycle is
   elapsed time and cannot be read as that hart's selected or accepted service.
   Forward risk for Phase4: commit_sel derives from ONE commit_instr_i.hart_id for all
   commit ports, which is safe only while the drained handoff keeps a group single-hart.
   REPAIRED and re-measured (smt2-pmu-banked-v2-20260919, model
   80ad8af04fa1491115ac1a3543c15bea6027992af82bc31f730ebd59bfb86113). perf_counters
   now banks generic_counter/mhpmevent/OF/MINH/SINH/UINH by a new `hart_i` index
   dimensioned from the existing CVA6Cfg.NrHarts, so no new config field appears and
   NH=1 collapses to the previous single bank. scountovf/lcofi became per-hart vectors
   routed to the matching CSR bank instead of broadcast, and because only the owning
   bank increments, a peer's HPM CSR write no longer suppresses this hart's counting.
   The same probe that found the defect now reports countersArePerHart=true with the
   banked signature (h0=1, h1=0): hart0 still reads back its OWN earlier write while
   hart1 never sees it, so isolation is shown in both directions. The shared signature
   (2,1) is now the failing control, alongside the value-corruption control. My first
   banked expectation of (0,0) was wrong and the run corrected it; hart0 legitimately
   keeps its own value. Cost is exactly (NH-1)*MHPMCounterNum*(64+8+4) = 456 added
   flops at NH=2; mapped area, timing and power are NOT measured. Chosen over the
   legal read-only-zero alternative because that would leave the second Linux CPU
   with no counters at all.
   Final source re-validated end to end on model
   d3b95654062fc27156360c9b612bbb13adcf1a7db4abce19c228557867dd3a93:
   opensbi-final-dual-20260919 strictDualPassed=true at12,765,628 cycles with
   333,635/8,932,406 retirements, plus smt2-final-{pmu,asym,balance,startup,lrsc}
   -20260919 all passing (countersArePerHart=true; asym ratios0.92859/0.92649).
   Separate RV32 repair: the user-mode hpmcounterNh read used `>` (so 0xC83 never
   matched) and subtracted the MACHINE base0xB83 from a USER address, indexing 258
   into a[1..6] array. Live in cv32a6_imac_sv32 (XLEN=32, PerfCounterEn=1, RVZihpm=1),
   which now lints clean remotely at85 warnings with no recorded baseline, so that is
   a pass and not a no-regression comparison. RV64 is unaffected: the branch body is
   `riscv::XLEN == 32` guarded and its only other statement sets read_access_exception,
   which perf_counters never reads or drives out. No directed RV32 counter test exists.
   mcycle checked against the spec of record and is NOT a defect: it counts "clock
   cycles executed by the processor core on which the hart is running", so per-bank
   core-cycle counting is conformant and simply is not a per-hart service metric.
   Counting itself measured (smt2-pmuevt-attrib-20260919): SMT_PMUEVT has both harts
   select "load accesses" in their own mhpmcounter3 and run quotas of256 and768 loads
   across a window spanning many handoffs. Deltas are EXACTLY256 and768 — own-hart
   only, no cross-hart inflation and no dual-commit undercount here. Bounds rather than
   an exact count are asserted because the event ORs commit ports; the ~1024
   shared/cross-attributed arm and a corruption arm both fail. Banking the selector is
   therefore backed by correct per-hart counting, not assumed from it.
   Cross-switch attribution then measured closed (smt2-pmumiss-isolation-20260919):
   hart0 strides64 times past the32KiB D$ while hart1 issues NO memory access but
   stays schedulable throughout. hart0=64 misses, hart1=EXACTLY0, and the arm that
   would accept a nonzero hart1 fails. This matches the RTL, where wt_dcache_missunit
   pulses miss_o on mshr_allocate and g6lc_icache on the accepted ifill — miss
   INITIATION, not refill completion — while the drained handoff additionally requires
   an empty scoreboard and no pending stores, so a demand memory op cannot span a
   switch. A one-cycle handoff boundary stays un-probed rather than proven impossible.
   Side result: 64 misses for64 strided accesses confirms that workload's stride really
   defeats the cache, so its ~7-cycle RTT is a fast-served MISS, not a hit, agreeing
   with the independent WT hit=00 evidence.
   A translate_off assertion fails loudly if a commit hart ever differs from the
   attributed hart, which is the Phase4 tripwire.
4. Reuse HPM event selection + SBI PMU/perf mappings only after that gap is closed.
   cva6 currently instantiates ONE perf_counters block; its counter/selector arrays
   have no hart dimension and switch-reason pulses do not feed it. Therefore current
   sim-only traces and the existing per-hart wording are NOT a Linux PMU contract.
   Phase1b must first resolve counter ownership, selector programming, per-hart
   privilege/inhibit/overflow routing, concurrent access, RV32 read atomicity and
   reset/context-switch semantics. Reuse a small programmable pool where possible;
   do not multiply all counters by NH by default or renumber published events.
   Closing the gap is a design choice to be made explicitly and costed: per-hart
   counter/selector/OF state (area scales with NH x MHPMCounterNum), or a smaller
   banked subset with the remainder architecturally read-only-zero, or hart-tagged
   event qualification into shared counters. Whichever is chosen, the `we_i` global
   count suppression and the shared LCOFI/OF routing must be resolved with it.
5. An optional later control surface should expose only coarse relative priority or
   resource class and its effective clamp/status, via a versioned privileged firmware
   interface with discovery. No raw IQ partition vectors or cycle-by-cycle choices
   exposed to userspace. Define task migration/save/restore, WFI/offline reset, rate
   limiting, access permissions and counter side-channel policy. Hints cannot override
   architectural ordering, memory permissions, exception precision or service floors.
   Absent/unsupported hints use the validated static policy; no new CSR or SBI ID is
   reserved in this pass.

Phase1b exits only after controlled contention/no-contention tests demonstrate each
counter's denominator, owner, cancellation behavior and privilege isolation. Phase6
policy promotion then requires an improvement in useful shared-core capacity within
per-role slowdown/tail bounds, not merely a more equal selected-cycle share. Phase9
must exercise real Linux affinity, migration, scheduling domains, perf access and
interrupt/lock/shootdown behavior. OpenSBI completion remains a separate mandatory
baseline gate. This pass changes the evaluation path first, not the RTL defaults.

#### C. Whole-path ownership and likely change-set map

| Concern | Live code seam | Required interaction contract |
|---|---|---|
| Hart admission/restart | core/smt/g6lc_thread_select.sv, g6lc_smt_pc_bank.sv; core/cva6.sv; fetch_B/frontend.sv | Stop new outgoing work, drain safely, retain instruction-start architectural resume; never let peer flush delete untracked work. |
| Allocate/execute/retire | core/issue_stage.sv, scoreboard.sv, commit_stage.sv; core/ooo/g6lc_ooo_dispatch.sv | Issue, execution completion, usable result, architectural commit and cancelled-slot drop are distinct events. |
| Rename/PRF | core/ooo/g6lc_rename.sv, g6lc_prf.sv; core/include/g6lc_core_types.svh | Map/free/busy/commit/checkpoint conservation; owner/class/generation match; audit fixed8-bit physical tags before pool expansion. |
| OoO scheduling/memory | core/ooo/g6lc_iq.sv, g6lc_rob.sv, g6lc_lsq.sv, g6lc_memdep.sv; LSU/store/AMO units | Ready work may issue out of order, retirement is precise per hart; stores/AMOs/fences cannot escape architectural authorization. |
| Speculation/prediction | core/frontend/g6lc_bp_ckpt.sv, g6lc_bp_ghist.sv, g6lc_bp_tage.sv, g6lc_bp_ittage.sv, g6lc_bp_statcor.sv; fetch_B | Prediction-time membership and resolve/train owner survive out-of-order resolution, bank switch, overflow and flush. |
| Cross-core coherence | corev_apu/coherence/g6lc_coherence_pkg.sv, g6lc_coherence_hub.sv, g6lc_inval_bus.sv, g6lc_snoop_filter.sv; g6lc_cluster.sv | Accepted writes retain invalidation obligations; selected targets and selected sources acknowledge exactly once; held AXI identities survive backpressure. |
| Hierarchy/stream service | corev_apu/l2_cache/g6lc_l2_top.sv, g6lc_l2_mshr.sv; l3_cache/g6lc_l3_top.sv, g6lc_l3_inclusive_inv.sv, g6lc_server_prefetcher.sv | Distinct request ownership, refill/error/fence/invalidation ordering and fair demand service; capacity is not actual concurrency. |

SMT harts sharing one physical L1 are NOT distinct coherence-hub clients. Cluster
invalidation arrays are indexed by physical core; mhartid uses core*NrHarts+local
hart. Preserve those distinct namespaces. The current cluster ties the hub LR
sideband inputs to zero: audit the real reservation path before claiming cluster
LR/SC tracking or wiring a new policy. A tracker PMU pulse is not the reservation
mechanism. Stream-plane evaluation means the actual selected load/store-to-hierarchy
path and workers; do not infer a standalone engine or throughput from the SKU name.

#### D. Stage/phase work order and exit gates

##### Phase 0 — freeze, truthful gates and current discrepancy closeout

Completed in scoped evidence: dual-hart HSM baseline; architectural restart/drain;
WT response ownership/freshness; in-order AMO readiness; timed WT-tag compiler
control; OoO invalid-WB qualification; rename formal elaboration repair.
Finish documenting current evidence and collecting final broad results. Resolve
fetch-IQ assertion/witness independently, preserving raw counterexamples. Use
bounded execution/cleanup for solvers rather than leaving orphan jobs or counting
an interrupted witness as PASS. Extend oracle unit tests for exit status, missing
model/input, unsupported config, trace ordering, empty metrics and missing workers.
No scheduler/cache defaults change in this phase.

##### Phase 1 — baseline dual-runnable service and saturation observability

Extend existing startup/soak and scheduler-review fixtures, not the pinned firmware.
Add compute/compute, compute/miss, branch/miss, stream/stream and WFI/IPI wake cases
with synchronized ROIs and role swaps. Reuse typed scoreboard/handoff observation;
add narrowly scoped counters only where service attribution is currently missing.
Leaf tests: NH1 identity; NH2 both ready; asymmetric blocking; quantum1/default;
held/aborted handoff; flush/trap collision; delayed stores; WFI entry/wake. Derive
fairness bounds from eligibility, policy and a bounded drain environment. Distinguish
an externally blocked core from ready-peer denial. Include wrong-hart/dropped-event
and forced-starvation negatives. Exit: checked per-hart results, non-vacuous service
metrics and observer equivalence; retain current policy until measured evidence
justifies one separately gated scheduling change.

Initial symmetric baseline now implemented without RTL/policy changes:
`smt2-balance-ordered-20260919` runs512 checked iterations per hart (2,048 ordered
body retirements each), RVC/norvc, solo0/solo1, an output-corruption negative and
observer-off equivalence. Shared and solo RVC instruction images are identical;
only the active-worker data mask differs. RVC common-window service is49.9264%/
50.0736%; maximum observed body-retirement cycle gap76 on each hart. Weighted
shared/solo speedup0.90234, worst-hart slowdown2.21795x. Balanced observed service
is not a throughput gain, a proved fairness bound or full saturation. Kernel and
RTT qualification remain absent. Nineteen tooling tests include empty/dropped/
duplicated/reordered/wrong-owner trace refusals.

Attribution observers now added and measured (`smt2-balance-attr-final-20260919`,
instrumented model2ce91d639b964cfb…). Two read-only sim-only observers: `[smt-sched]`
edge-compressed active/ready/dmiss/imiss/block/quiesce/hold/trap/flush plus paired
decide/switch/abort with the drain wait, and `[smt-rtt]` load request/response/kill
tagged by the load buffer id. Plusargs smt_sched_trace/smt_rtt_trace, allowlisted;
the observer additions were verified inside translate_off by source inspection;
that was not a synthesized-netlist equivalence proof. Parsers reject non-monotonic
state, unmatched or mis-counted handoffs,
reissued tags and a dead observer;27 tooling tests pass.

RVC window (5,665 cycles) findings:
- served2834/2831, ready-denied2831/2834, never-not-ready0/0. Both harts are
  runnable the whole window, so this is pure time-slicing, not latency overlap.
  Served cycles agree with the independently measured retirement split.
- longest continuous denial of a ready hart68 cycles on each hart.
- **all83 handoffs fired on anti-starvation; quantum0, miss0, abort0.** The
  hybrid policy's quantum is effectively dead in this regime, confirming the
  units/precedence hazard recorded below: starvation counts waiting cycles and
  wins before the fetch-quantum count is reached. "Tune the quantum" is therefore
  not a viable first adaptive lever; the starvation limit is the operative control.
- quiesce332 cycles (5.86% of the window); every drain waited exactly3 cycles
  (83 samples, total249, max3). These measure selected residency and quiescence,
  not useful grants or refetch loss. They do NOT fully explain the2.218x slowdown;
  causal attribution of the remainder needs fetch/issue/retirement measurements.
- The RTT observer is alive (23 events) but records **zero in-window samples**: the
  fixed-work body is register-only. **Cache RTT is therefore still unmeasured**, and
  no latency-driven conclusion may be drawn from this workload.

Current asymmetric pilot `smt2-asym-rtt-20260919` executes checked compute/memory
roles and reports64 load RTTs per memory ROI. Its interpretation requires correction:
hart0 polls after finishing while hart1 parks, contaminating the role-swap compute
tail. Fix completion with a last-publisher election and matched solo controls on
BOTH harts before using these times for placement/policy decisions. Separate the
common overlap window from the batch and each per-hart ROI; do not count a busy-wait
reporter as productive work. Scheduler active cycles will be labelled selected,
not service/utilization, and no 50/50 acceptance threshold applies to asymmetric work.

A 7-cycle response is NOT evidence of a hit: sampled retained WT lookups have hit=00
and no forwarding, while some 1-cycle samples forward seed stores. The 64KiB span
only touches64 lines, but a1024-byte stride also concentrates set pressure; byte
footprint alone cannot predict hits. Retain the seeded-state setup and actual model
identity; report load-port RTT without inventing L1/L2/DRAM attribution or a memory
bandwidth stress qualification. Add cache-state/service-latency controls separately.

Lock contention now measured (`smt2-lock-holder-v2-20260919`, model d3b95654...).
SMT_LOCK gives both harts one lock and identical512-iteration critical sections; the
oracle checks the sections never overlap AND times them, with same-hart solo controls.
Mutual exclusion holds (hart0550-5395, hart1 5451-7598, disjoint). Holder cost:
hart0's section takes4846 cycles contended versus2066 solo, a2.3456x slowdown, while
hart1 spins and produces NOTHING. hart1's own later section is2148 versus2066 solo
(1.0397x) because by then the peer had finished, which confirms the asymmetry comes
from spinning rather than a general sharing tax.

This is the decisive argument against equalizing sibling service: the spinner consumed
roughly57% of the holder's capacity to produce zero useful work, and total useful work
on the core would have been strictly higher had it yielded. It also sharpens the
earlier caution — a low-IPC hart may be a lock HOLDER worth protecting, but a spinning
WAITER is the one that should yield. That argues for a yield/pause-aware admission hint
before any IPC- or RTT-derived throttle, and it must key on an explicit software hint
(zihintpause/WFI), never on inferred spin patterns or lock addresses.

Dependency-limited pairing measured (`smt2-dep-ceiling-v5-20260919`). SMT_DEP reuses the
asymmetric oracle with roles `dep` (three multiplies chained through one register per
iteration) and `ind` (same instruction count, independent destinations), 512 iterations,
solo controls per role per hart. Premise confirmed: solo dep=4123 cycles, ind=3108 for
2560 body retirements, i.e. 0.62 vs 0.82 IPC, so real issue slack exists. Result: batch
ratio 0.8985 in BOTH role orders — not just below 1 but WORSE than compute/memory
(0.929/0.926). ROI windows overlap 6872 of 8048 cycles while instruction-level overlap
stays zero.

This is the key negative result for the arbitration work: the canonical SMT win case
returns nothing, so no fetch weighting, quantum or starvation-limit tuning can help.
Capturing idle issue slots requires both harts RESIDENT and issuing simultaneously,
which is the Phase4 mixed-residency prerequisite. Biased fetch and RS partitioning are
therefore worthless until Phase4 lands; only the yield/pause hint (from the lock result)
pays off before then. The plan's ordering should reflect that.

Idle-sibling and IPI wake measured (`smt2-ipi-wake-20260919`). SMT_IPI parks hart1 in
WFI (MSIE set, mstatus.MIE clear so it resumes in place), runs hart0's fixed body
beside it, then wakes the peer via its own CLINT MSIP at 0x02000004; hart0 asserts its
OWN mip.MSIP stays clear, so per-hart routing is checked not assumed. The oracle also
refuses any run where the halted peer retires measured work, with a unit test proving
that check bites. Result: hart0 takes 2082 cycles with the sibling halted vs 2061 solo
= **1.0102x**, peer retired zero body instructions, IPI woke only its target.

Phase1 workload set is now complete, and it locates the cost precisely:
- halted sibling: 1.01x, effectively free — no mechanism needed
- spinning sibling: 2.16x, recoverable to 1.37x by the implemented PAUSE hint
- genuinely runnable sibling: ~2x per hart, batch 0.90-0.93, zero overlap gain

The shared-core penalty is therefore entirely time-slicing a RUNNABLE sibling, not SMT
structure. This confirms the ordering above: the only remaining upside is Phase4 mixed
residency, plus the yield hint already landed. Admission/fetch tuning has nothing to
win. Memory-service sweep done (`smt2-memsweep-d{16,64,256}-20260919`, RTT variants
`-rtt-d{16,256}-`). SMT2_REVIEW_MEM_DEPTH retargets the memory role (stride1024 over a
256KiB buffer, expected sum recomputed) so miss count sweeps with all else fixed:

| depth | ratio |
|---:|---|
|16 |0.9557 /0.9551 |
|64 |0.9286 /0.9265 |
|256|0.9028 /0.9057 |

Sharing degrades MONOTONICALLY with memory pressure — the opposite of the textbook SMT
expectation. The RTT observer rules out interference: median and max load latency are
7 cycles in EVERY configuration (depth16 or256, shared or solo). Latency does not
grow; the memory role's serialised stall time does, and none of it overlaps.

This sizes the Phase4 opportunity: ~7 idle cycles per iteration x256 iterations is
roughly1800 cycles of idle issue inside a 6853-cycle shared run. Existing switch-on-miss
cannot claim it — MISS_STALL_THRESH is 32 SUSTAINED stall cycles with a 16-cycle
blackout, so a 7-cycle stall never qualifies, and lowering the threshold would not help
because a drained handoff costs ~3 cycles plus refetch and would have to drain straight
back. Only mixed residency can use a 7-cycle hole.

Phase1 is now closed. Phase2/3 in progress.

**FULL-CORE OoO DOES NOT SYNTHESIZE (2026-09-19)** — found by building the legal
baseline the user then directed. `core/include/g6lc64_ooo_int_config_pkg.sv`
(OoOEn=1, NrHarts=1, all FP formats cleared so FpPresent=0) is the FIRST full-core OoO
configuration that legally elaborates; registered in .licensing-tiers (R), REUSE.toml,
a diag-ooo-int-lint diagnostic and warningBaselineRemote=11.

Lint passes (11 warnings, no guard). **Synthesis FAILS: 435 `check -assert` problems**,
a combinational cycle between `issue_read_operands.issue_ack` and
`i_ooo_dispatch.i_iq.issue_ack_i`.

CONFIRMED REAL: the gate runs check -assert after only `proc; opt -fast`, and yosys
advises `-force-detailed-loop-check` to rule out false positives. That precise run
(a one-off check -assert -force-detailed-loop-check, ~30min; gate left unchanged) reported **497 problems,
MORE than 435**, so the finding survives the strictest check rather than dissolving.

Mechanism: g6lc_iq reads `issue_ack_i[grants]` INSIDE its selection loop (low ack sets
grants=NrIssuePorts to preserve age order) while issue_read_operands derives
`issue_ack_o[p]` from the entry the IQ just selected. Pre-existing and structural: the
component fixture synthesizes at zero SCCs only because it ties issue_ack_i to 2'b11.

**FIXED (same day): select-then-confirm, not a registered ack.** g6lc_iq presents the
oldest ready candidates as a pure function of queue state and consumes issue_ack_i only
in a separate removal block driving next-state, so the ack leaves the selection cone.
No issue cycle lost.

Removal drops EXACTLY the acked ports, not a prefix. A prefix rule was written first
and was wrong — with an earlier port un-acked it keeps an entry whose own port WAS
acked, which the consumer already took, so it would issue again next cycle. The
randomised IQ fixture (scenario4 drives deliberately non-prefix ack patterns) caught
it. Exact-ack removal is correct for any pattern and assumes nothing about the
consumer, which matters because NrIssuePorts can exceed1 with SuperscalarEn=0. Under
SuperscalarEn behaviour is unchanged.

RESULT on g6lc64_ooo_int: lint 11 warnings (baseline), **synth CLEAN (2 warnings)**,
down from 435 problems / 497 detailed. Regressions: 24/24 IQ cases including the
randomised ack scenario; dispatch default set, 11-14, 15-17 and scenario19 pass, with
REVIEW_RTL_LATERESULT_FAULT=1 still failing as required.

**PHASE4 STEP1 LANDED: per-hart rename namespaces.** g6lc_rename gains NR_HARTS
(default1); map_q/amap_q are [hart][arch]; hart_i per dispatch port and commit_hart_i
per commit port; free_q/busy_q UNCHANGED and shared. Checkpoints record their owning
hart so recovery restores only that hart's map, and ckpt_alloc is marked only for
same-hart allocations — otherwise a peer's allocation after this branch would be
squashed by this branch's mispredict, reintroducing the aliasing through the RECOVERY
path rather than the map. x0 never consults the map, so physical0 stays the shared
zero; reset gives hart h physicals 31*h+1..31*h+31, pool from 31*NH+1. NR_HARTS=1
reproduces the old bindings exactly and g6lc64_smt2 rebuilds BYTE-IDENTICAL.
**Ownership + per-hart flush landed**: owner_q[phys] records the owning hart, which is
what makes flush_hart_i[h] possible (the shared freelist records no owner). Per-hart
flush restores that hart's map from its own amap, returns every physical it owns that
its committed map no longer references, and clears its checkpoints — peer untouched.
**Tested at NrHarts=2 WITHOUT relaxing the guard**: g6lc_rename is package-free, so the
fixture instantiates it at NR_HARTS=2 (-GNR_HARTS=2 -GPRF_ENTRIES=80 -GPRF_W=7).
Scenarios 11/12/13 = namespace isolation / independent recovery / per-hart flush, with
negative controls RENAME_HART_ALIAS / RENAME_PEER_SQUASHED / RENAME_HART_FLUSH_SPILL;
all 6 cases match. First genuine NrHarts>1 evidence in the OoO path. Remaining 3
planned tests need IQ/ROB/LSQ hart tagging.

Verified: rename/dispatch/drop/late-result fixtures, rename formal proof + its
checker-negative (mutation re-pointed to map_q[0][0]), g6lc64_ooo_int lint 11 at
baseline and synth clean. GUARD UNTOUCHED — check_cfg still refuses OoOEn&&NrHarts>1.
Remaining before the guard can move: IQ/ROB/LSQ hart tagging, per-hart cancellation,
and physical-register OWNERSHIP for per-hart flush.

**Phase4 (NrHarts>1) scoped with a verification plan**, grounded in g6lc_rename state.
The guard still refuses OoO+SMT and the fixture negative confirms it after every change
in this pass (REVIEW_RTL_ILLEGAL=smt, -GHARTS=2, must fail to build — it does). A
per-hart test cannot be written yet because g6lc_rename has NO hart signal to address.
- map_q/amap_q are ONE namespace -> per hart (the aliasing).
- free_q/busy_q are shared and STAY shared: a shared physical pool is what makes SMT
  cheaper than two cores, and busy is a property of a physical register.
- ckpt_map_q per hart or hart-tagged, so a hart-1 mispredict cannot roll back hart0.
- rs1/rs2/rd and commit_rd need an accompanying hart id.
HARD PART = per-hart flush: the shared freelist records no owner, so parking one hart
cannot reclaim its physicals without new ownership state. Do NOT split the freelist to
dodge it — that discards the shared-pool utilisation that justifies SMT.
Tests, each with a failing control: (1) namespace isolation (both harts' own x5 —
headline gate, fails today by construction); (2) independent recovery; (3) no double
allocation from the shared freelist; (4) per-hart commit; (5) per-hart flush reclaiming
exactly that hart's physicals; (6) the per-hart late-WB invariant from the TID-reuse
finding — each FU's suppression must hold PER HART, not just per core.

Still NOT a finished product: no independent-reference comparison, no boot, no FP.
Guards reframed: Phase5 makes g6lc64_ooo ELABORATE; it was never what stopped the
integrated core from SYNTHESIZING.

Superseded decision, retained for history —
**(2026-09-19, user): do not add an integer-only OoO configuration.** The
elaboration guard stays as the intended refusal; a legal full-core OoO baseline waits
for the redesigns that make an EXISTING target legal rather than a new config carved
around the restriction. No SKU, no verification-only package.

Ordering consequence, from checking which guard actually fires: `g6lc64_ooo` already
sets NrHarts=1, so the hart guard passes and the SINGLE failure is
`assert(!(Cfg.OoOEn && Cfg.FpPresent))` (the package sets RVF=RVD=1). Therefore
**Phase5 (OoO FP register class) ALONE makes g6lc64_ooo legal** and unblocks the
full-core OoO baseline plus its independent-reference comparison. Phase4 per-hart
namespaces are NOT required for that target; Phase4 is what g6lc64_ooo_server needs
(NrHarts=2 trips the hart guard as well) and what mixed residency needs. The two are
independent and neither blocks the other, so they can proceed in either order.

**Phase5 scoped from verified source.** g6lc_rename.sv has NO FP notion at all (no
is_rd_fpr / fpr / FpPresent): rename, busy table and freelist treat every destination
as one integer class, so the work is additive. Items:
1. Rename keys on {is_fpr, rd} = 64 architectural entries. The asymmetry that must not
   be flattened: integer x0 is hardwired zero and the code leans on physical 0 meaning
   "no destination", while FP f0 is an ordinary writable register — the `prd != 0` test
   becomes class-aware.
2. FMA reads rs3. issue_read_operands already carries rs3/rs3_fpr/operand_c_regfile,
   but g6lc_ooo_dispatch reads only NP*2 operands; FP needs NP*3 PRF read ports, on top
   of the commit write port just added.
3. The new commit mirror is wired to we_gpr_i only; commit_stage drives a separate
   we_fpr_o, so FP destinations would not be mirrored. Deliberate while FP is refused
   at elaboration, but it is Phase5 work and must not be forgotten.
4. FP is FLen wide vs an XLEN PRF — equal for RV64D, not for RVF-only RV64.
5. fflags ordering against OoO retirement needs its own check.

**PHASE5 STEP1 LANDED**: split FP register class in g6lc_rename behind FPRF_ENTRIES
(0 = disabled, bit-identical). Own maps/pool/busy/ports per class; checkpoints stay
SHARED because a branch is one program-order event. The split is vindicated by the
diff: every integer path guards rd!=0/prd!=0 and reserves physical0, while every FP
path deliberately has none — f0 is an ordinary writable register. Capacity is per
pool, so FP cannot stall out integer allocation. Verified: rename + NR_HARTS=2 +
dispatch fixtures, rename formal + negative, g6lc64_smt2 BYTE-IDENTICAL.

**STEP2 CONSTRAINTS VERIFIED BEFORE CODING:**
1. rs3 has no field — its index rides in `sbe.result[4:0]`, and is_imm_fpr covers
   FADD:FSUB as well as FMADD:FNMADD, so it is the common FP-compute case. The
   overload is disjoint by FU (AGU uses result as the immediate only for LOAD/STORE;
   FP loads are is_rd_fpr but not is_imm_fpr). So dispatch must rename result[4:0]
   through the FP map and deliver the VALUE on a new issue_op_c_o path — it cannot
   ride back in `result`, which still holds the register number.
2. The in-order FP regfile has THREE read ports TOTAL, muxed to whichever issue port
   holds the FP op: only one FP instruction reads per cycle today. DECISION: mirror
   that (3 muxed ports) rather than 3 per issue port — measured PRF port cost makes
   the wide version expensive, and it would smuggle a performance change into a
   correctness feature.

Earlier open design choice, now settled as SPLIT: unified PRF (one freelist and write-port
set, but widened entries and the f0 exception threaded through shared logic) versus
split int/FP files (keeps the integer path bit-identical — which matters because
OoOEn=0 must stay bit-identical and the integer OoO path is the one now under test —
at the cost of a second freelist and recovery structure).

**Second gate CONFIRMED AS A DEFECT: CSR and LR results never reach the PRF under
OoOEn=1** (`ooo-lateresult-confirmed-20260919`, matched=true vs expected error
DISPATCH_LATERESULT; tb_g6lc_review_dispatch scenario19 via REVIEW_RTL_LATERESULT=1).
The producer writes back only placeholder 0x0BAD, commit supplies architectural 0x1234,
and the renamed consumer READ 0x0BAD. g6lc_ooo_dispatch writes the PRF only from the execute
writeback bus (`prf_wdata[w]=wb_data_i[w]`) with no commit-time PRF write, while
commit_stage substitutes the architectural result at COMMIT into the ARCHITECTURAL
regfile (`wdata_o[0]=csr_rdata_i`, `=amo_resp_i.result`), never touching wb_data_i.
Every dispatched instruction is renamed with no CSR/AMO exclusion, and renamed
consumers read the PRF. No commit flush covers it: flush_commit_o is asserted for SC
and RMW but EXPLICITLY NOT for LR, and not for CSR at all. So a consumer renamed
against a CSR read or an LR destination gets the execute-stage placeholder with no
flush to squash it — the same defect class as the in-order LR bug already repaired,
which the in-order fix does not cover for the OoO path.

Reachability verified end to end, including issue_read_operands selecting
`(OoOEn && ooo_renamed) ? ooo_op_a_i : rdata`, so the PRF operand overrides the
architectural regfile read. Unlike the TID-reuse item below this is real on all three
counts: fixture-confirmed mechanism, verified reachability, and a masking invariant
actively searched for and found explicitly absent for exactly CSR and LR.

**REPAIRED.** g6lc_ooo_dispatch mirrors the architectural commit write into the PRF:
issue_stage feeds it the same we_gpr_i/wdata_i the architectural regfile receives, keyed
by `commit_prd[c]` — the committing instruction's OWN physical register, NOT a trans_id,
which would have reintroduced exactly the aliasing the TID-reuse invariant prevents.
g6lc_prf gives the highest write port priority, so the mirror is appended after the
execute writebacks and architectural truth wins a same-address clash. Mirroring every
committed write rather than decoding CSR/AMO keeps the contract future-proof.

No exclusivity proof existed for reusing an execute writeback port at commit, so a port
was added and its cost MEASURED rather than assumed. Port count is the driver, so the
mirror is ONE port: only commit port0 can carry a commit-produced value
(`wdata_o[0]` from csr_rdata_i / amo_resp_i.result), while port1 writes
commit_instr[1].result which the execute writeback already delivered; a second mirror
port appears only under RVZacas (0 here).

| Variant | Generic cells |
|---|---:|
| previously recorded baseline |86,824 |
| one mirror port (shipped) |**94,505** |
| one per commit port |97,439 |

The 1-vs-2 comparison is a paired measurement (2,934 cells saved); the baseline is from
an earlier run of the same fixture, so the absolute delta is indicative only. State
stays 6,250 bits, zero latches/SCCs. Generic-cell fixture, not mapped PPA.

Verified: scenario19 passes; REVIEW_RTL_LATERESULT_FAULT=1 restores the defect and it
fails again; scenarios11-14,15-17 and the default dispatch set with negatives pass;
the TID-reuse probe is unchanged; and g6lc64_smt2 rebuilds BYTE-IDENTICAL
(ba1f8f6a722829aa…), so the OoOEn=0 path and the dual-hart boot gate are untouched.

First gate, for contrast:

**Late writeback after trans_id reuse** (`ooo-tidreuse-confirmed-20260919`, matched=true
against expected error DISPATCH_TIDREUSE). New tb_g6lc_review_dispatch scenario18 via
REVIEW_RTL_TIDREUSE=1. g6lc_ooo_dispatch qualifies writebacks only with
`!cancelled_mask_i[wb_id_i[w]]`, indexed by trans_id, so once a cancelled id is dropped
and reallocated its cancel bit clears and a late result for the OLD owner is
indistinguishable from the NEW owner's completion. Measured: stale 0xDEAD woke the new
owner's consumer and was supplied as its operand. Worse than a spurious wake — the
dropped victim's physical register is correctly freed and reallocated (scenario16
proves that free is right), so the stale result lands on the live owner's own register.
My initial setup assertion that those registers must differ was wrong and was removed.

**Superseded by the current pre-grant cancellation increment:** the survey below
missed requests queued before cache grant. Those requests do produce a late reused-
TID writeback in the frozen firmware. Preserve the historical probe but not the
unreachability claim.

**Historical correction after surveying writeback sources: this was classified as a LATENT CONTRACT
DEPENDENCY, not a live defect, with no repair proposed at dispatch.** Nothing in the
current OoO configuration can produce the injected stimulus:
- `load_unit` keeps a STICKY per-slot `ldbuf_flushed_q` (all slots on flush, TID-matching
  slots on cancelled_mask_i) and frees a slot ONLY when its response actually returns,
  so a flushed load's slot cannot be reallocated while outstanding;
- `mult`/`serdiv` gate on `~flush_i`, and serdiv disables its register enables on flush;
- ALU/branch/CSR have no multi-cycle in-flight state;
- CvxifEn=0 in g6lc64_ooo_config_pkg, so there is no external late-result source.

The load unit therefore already implements repair option2 one level down, which makes
option2 redundant at dispatch and option1 (a generation tag on every FU writeback)
unjustified for a hazard nothing can produce. Investigating before coding avoided
widening every FU interface for no benefit.

The real finding is the HIDDEN COUPLING. Dispatch qualifies writebacks only with
`!cancelled_mask_i[wb_id_i[w]]`, released when the id is dropped, so it silently
depends on an invariant it neither states nor checks:

  NO FUNCTIONAL UNIT MAY PRESENT A WRITEBACK FOR A FLUSHED OR CANCELLED trans_id,
  enforced with state that SURVIVES the release of cancelled_mask_i.

Dispatch structurally cannot detect the reuse case — once the id belongs to a live
issued instruction, any per-TID "awaiting writeback" flag is set for the new owner too,
so only an end-to-end generation tag could distinguish. That is precisely the cost of
relaxing the invariant, and the reason to keep it.

Phase4 consequence: per-hart cancellation and mixed residency must preserve this
invariant per hart, and any new writeback source (CVXIF, accelerator) must be checked
against it. Scenario18 is retained as a contract probe recorded as an expected failure,
and is the regression that would catch a future FU that stops suppressing flushed
results. OoOEn=0 stays default; no configured target is affected. Anti-starvation triggers dominate the current workloads, but changing their
limit is only a candidate. Raising the current fetch quantum alone cannot help;
shortening it enough could alter the reason mix. No knob is promoted before B1's
useful-capacity, forward-service and tail constraints pass.

Completed measurement correction: smt2-core-capacity{-norvc,}-20260919 reuses model
2ce91d... with atomic last-publisher completion, both roles on both solo harts and
identical instruction bytes per encoding.12 positive cases/2 result negatives/4
observer-off equivalence replays pass;36 unit tests. RVC core-batch cycles3767/3782
versus summed same-hart solo ROIs3498/3504 yield ratios0.92859/0.92649. Norvc ratios
0.92574/0.92733 agree in direction: no throughput case for pairing this workload.
The swapped RVC compute ROI changes5743->3782 after the harness correction, not a
hardware gain. All memory ROIs have64 RTT samples and compute ROIs zero own loads.
Selected residency is labelled separately from accepted work, and outstanding ages
are sampled at the requested window boundary. Per-role, shared-window and physical-
core denominators are distinct. No policy default, hardware hint ABI or DUT changes.
Symmetric/startup/LRSC regressions re-pass with negatives on the same model
(smt2-capacity-{balance,startup,lrsc}-regression-20260919). Default remote lint passes
at8/54 warnings and synthesis at32/5; SMT2 lint passes with1 warning (no warning
baseline) and synthesis at31. Overall verification remains INCOMPLETE: strict
standalone-slang elaboration is unavailable on the builder. No skip override,
assertion waiver or repository verification-policy change was made. Fetch-IQ's
previous formal failure is still open and was not rerun or reclassified here.

##### Phase 2 — finish single-hart OoO architectural event ownership

First bounded change set is now repaired and live-port qualified: cancelled
commit/drop previously poisoned the committed map, freed an owned physical register
and released a replacement checkpoint. g6lc_ooo_dispatch now qualifies map/free/
checkpoint/store-commit strobes with commit_ack AND not-cancelled, keeping raw ROB
retirement separate. Cases15–17 reproduce all three defects before the repair.
Single-lane and paired older-live/younger-cancelled retirement pass six positives,
six checker negatives and six single-mechanism fault controls. Artifacts:
ooo-drop-{before,after,map,free,checkpoint}-20260919 and
ooo-drop-dual-{after,map,free,checkpoint}-20260919. Full flush after drop and
replacement-checkpoint reuse are checked; simultaneous full flush with older
architectural commit, full-core traps and general TID generation remain further gates.
No namespace/FP guard is removed.

Next distinct increment: CSR/AMO late results. Early execution WB may be commit-ready
but not operand-ready. Trace actual commit data and write enables from commit_stage
through issue_stage to architectural RF and PRF. Introduce a typed data-ready event
or proven serialization/port-sharing mechanism; do not just mask early wakeup and
leave the PRF permanently stale. Prove port exclusivity before borrowing an existing
write port; otherwise quantify the cost of a new port. Test LR->branch/ALU/store,
SC success/failure, AMO RMW, CSR read/write, exception and mispredict cancellation.

Then close lifetime after TID reuse/full flush. Either quarantine a slot until every
old completion is accounted for, or propagate an allocation generation end to end.
A cancelled bit cannot distinguish an old response after the slot has been reused;
one epoch bit is not sufficient without a proved wrap/lifetime bound. Trace this
through long-latency FU, load responses, scoreboard, rename and PRF. Exit: legal
single-hart integer full-core checks against an independent architectural oracle;
leaf ownership alone does not enable the named FP/OoO packages.

##### Phase 3 — legal OoO integration baseline and cost characterization

Construct an explicitly isolated legal integer OoO test profile (not a silent SKU
or ISA-string change); verify config/type/flist boundaries and refusal controls.
Exercise program-order retirement, WAW/WAR/RAW, branch recovery, traps, fences,
self-modifying code, memory aliases, cacheable/MMIO separation and constrained LR/SC.
Use both instruction encodings, several queue depths/port counts including minimum
and non-power-of-two cases where supported. Demand completion data and exceptions
must agree with a reference, not merely a cookie. Synthesize the actually enabled
backend and whole core: disabled-OoO default synthesis is not this gate. Keep area
categories explicit (logic, state, macros, metadata, blackboxes), and record critical
wake/select/bypass and commit/recovery cones. Exit: a real enabled baseline before
throughput tuning, SMT namespace enablement or FP-class promotion.

##### Phase 4 — per-hart OoO namespace design, then coarse-handoff integration

Anticipated interfaces/types: hart ownership in rename requests/commits/WB; hart-
indexed speculative and architectural maps; owner-aware PRF allocation; checkpoint
head/count, saved maps and tags keyed by hart/slot/generation; IQ/ROB/LSQ/memdep and
cancel masks carrying hart identity. Audit every use of trans_id, rd[4:0], commit_ptr,
global flush and p_rs/p_rd. Core/smt GPR/CSR/PC banking alone does not bank the OoO
committed map or PRF. Budget tags and check configuration bounds before widening.

Compare deterministic per-hart PRF partitions against a shared pool with owner
metadata and guaranteed per-hart credits. Integer x0 may share one immutable zero
physical register; other committed identities cannot alias. Baseline capacity is
at least1+31*NH plus speculative headroom for an integer shared pool, with a
separate budget for FP. Do not duplicate large arrays merely for convenient indexing.
Document lifetime, context reset/debug access and physical port/bank implications.

First support the EXISTING drained handoff while preserving each inactive hart's
committed values; do not globally reset rename to identity on a switch. Test same
architectural destinations with different values, peer halt/wake/trap, checkpoint
reuse, memory responses across switches and per-hart precise restart. Only after
these pass consider lifting the SMT guard for this explicit supported envelope.

A later subphase may allow mixed-hart resident work: choose per-hart retirement
heads/ROB partitions or another proved per-hart ordered policy, with allocation and
issue quotas. A global FIFO is a valid conservative scaffold but can cause peer
head-of-line blocking; do not call it ideal SMT scheduling. Test peer progress while
one hart is blocked, and ensure a global FU/cache flush cannot silently kill the peer.

##### Phase 5 — OoO FP register class, independently then with SMT

Add class identity for sources/destinations through decoded/scoreboard types,
rename maps, busy/ready, PRF, IQ wakeup, bypass, WB and commit. Audit/add p_rs3 and
three-source readiness for fused FP operations; current p_rs1/p_rs2/p_rd fields
are not sufficient evidence of FP support. f0 is writable and must not inherit x0
suppression. Preserve FLEN/XLEN and NaN-boxing rules and mixed integer/FP moves,
conversions, compare/classify, loads/stores, dynamic rounding and per-hart frm/fflags.
FP exception flags commit only for the surviving owner; cancelled long-latency
FPU results must not wake or corrupt a reused register. CVXIF/vector destinations
are separate class/ownership contracts, not implicitly covered by scalar FP.

Prefer class-separated storage/ports when it avoids widening every integer cell;
measure bank conflicts and FMA read-port cost. Keep existing macro/gating/DFT seams.
Qualify single-hart FP first, then two-hart coarse scheduling, then mixed resident
integer/FP pressure. Remove the FP guard only for tested supported configurations,
with compile-time refusals for the remaining illegal combinations.

##### Phase 6 — speculation, branch prediction and scheduling co-interactions

Stable branch identity must connect prediction-time snapshots, OoO resolution,
commit-time checkpoint release, restore and training. A FIFO-only youngest fallback
is not a general solution to out-of-order or stale resolves. Test two branches
resolving in reverse order, overflow/desync, same-cycle allocate/resolve/commit,
RVC boundaries, opposite outcomes in one fetch window, peer-bank updates and redirects.
Keep architectural restart distinct from speculative fetch cursor and branch-history
snapshots. Shared BHT/BTB capacity is a performance/isolation choice; bank or tag
only what correctness/ownership requires before paying for full replication.

Tune selection only after Phase1 metrics and Phase4 ownership: separate admission,
issue and fetch arbitration; retain fairness under shared PRF/IQ/LSQ pressure and
FU backpressure. Evaluate quantum/RR/miss policy changes one at a time, including
handoff cost and worst-hart slowdown. Predictor accuracy gains must survive actual
accepted/retired-work checks; lookup alignment or a counter does not prove a gain.

###### Phase6 adaptive-policy extension — hypotheses, not enabled RTL

Correctness and policy are separate layers. The legal candidate set is first formed
from operand readiness, owner/class/generation, resource credit, architectural
ordering, exception/flush and hart eligibility. Adaptation may rank or budget only
that set; it never overrides these predicates. Admission throttles apply to NEW
work, not already accepted operations, outstanding responses, store draining,
invalidation completion or interrupt delivery. Retain the static policy as a
config-gated fallback and leave the validated SMT2 default unchanged.

The current drained-handoff design cannot hide an outgoing long load by issuing
the peer before that load drains. Therefore a cache-miss signal alone is not evidence
that adaptive switching will hide RTT. First target unnecessary refetch/switch cost
and denied runnable service. True cross-hart latency overlap requires Phase4 mixed-
resident ownership and precise recovery; do not remove the drain to manufacture it.

Candidate sequence, cheapest mechanism first:

1. Static RR/quantum plus explicit fairness/minimum-credit floor. Establish a
   non-adaptive reference under every workload mix, including one runnable hart.
2. Bounded quantum adjustment at safe handoff boundaries: lengthen when repeated
   handoffs mostly pay drain/refetch cost; shorten only when an eligible peer gains
   verified useful service. Preserve priority for halt/wake and the hard fairness
   floor. Evaluate hysteresis before changing defaults.
3. After namespace qualification, occupancy/pressure-aware allocation caps for
   IQ/ROB/PRF/LSQ/MSHR. Compare fixed partitions with a shared pool plus guaranteed
   minimums. Borrow only free capacity; never revoke live allocations or evict state
   merely to meet a new quota. Account for a dependency producer/lock holder, not
   just the consumer's low IPC, before penalizing a hart.
4. RTT/MLP-aware bias as a separate optional candidate. A memory-heavy hart can
   benefit from more independent requests, but can also monopolize shared queues
   and harm a peer. Decide from measured marginal useful progress and congestion,
   not from "long RTT -> throttle" or "long RTT -> switch" as universal rules.
5. Demand/prefetch budget coordination only after the demand policy is sound.
   Preserve demand/completion/invalidation escape credits and cross-core fairness;
   independent aggressive controllers must not create cyclic credit waits.

Use small saturating per-hart counters and quantized latency/pressure buckets,
shift-based smoothing, bounded additive policy steps and a slow update window.
Compute the next policy outside the combinational wake/select/ready loop; update
registered policy state only at defined safe boundaries. Avoid multipliers,
dividers, table replication or a speculative learned policy in the critical path.
Define minimum sample count, cold-start fallback, minimum residency, hysteresis,
clamps and anti-windup for a blocked peer. Empty/noisy metrics must select the static
policy, not an invented zero-latency or infinite-productivity result. Reset/flush/
hart-offline semantics and policy-state DFT/PMU exposure are part of the design.
New controls need CVA6Cfg fields, legality assertions, minimal-config identity and
an explicit area/state/timing budget before implementation; names/widths are not yet
frozen. Do not advertise autonomy, stability or gains before controlled evidence.

Cache RTT measurement must distinguish: accepted fetch request->delivered response;
load issue->usable result; accepted D-cache transaction->data; MSHR allocation->fill;
and memory return->architectural commit. These contain different queue, replay,
translation, interconnect and execution costs. Carry hart and transaction generation;
separate hit/miss level, demand/prefetch, same-line waiter versus fill, cancellation,
error and retry where interfaces expose them. Do not infer L1/L2/L3 level solely
from latency bins. Report outstanding ages and cancelled/censored samples alongside
completed distributions to avoid survivor bias. Correlate RTT, queue occupancy,
useful IPC, backpressure and bank conflicts under a declared memory service model.
Use N_required approximately target_completion_rate * measured_RTT only as a
conditional outstanding-request budget; dependency chains, fill serialization,
bandwidth, bank ports and response credits can make that concurrency unattainable.
More MSHRs do not create independent requests or remove a serialized controller.

Audit policy precedence and units before tuning: the current hybrid selector prefers
a starved peer before its quantum condition; SmtStarveLimit counts waiting cycles,
whereas SmtFetchQuantum counts accepted fetches. The current64/128 values therefore
cannot be interpreted as two comparable residency limits. This is now measured, not
hypothesised: on the symmetric dual-runnable workload every observed handoff fires on
starvation and none on quantum, so a quantum change would be inert there. Re-measure
the switch-reason mix on each new workload before attributing any effect to a knob.
An ideal/clairvoyant scheduling model is at most an upper bound under its declared
dependency/service assumptions, not deployable RTL or a Linux correctness oracle.

Linux cross-behavior gates (after a real legal enabled-core baseline):

- Topology and scheduler-domain truth: ariane-smt2.dts already describes thread0/1
  under one physical core. Validate what the running kernel actually exposes as
  sibling CPUs/shared caches; keep mhartid, CLINT/PLIC routing and feature discovery
  aligned with the candidate. Pin task affinity and record migration/offline/idle
  conditions. Hardware policy does not know Linux task identity or substitute for
  the kernel scheduler. Do not alter the frozen boot DTB for this experiment.
- Precise timer/external/software interrupts and per-hart IPI delivery, idle/WFI
  wakeup, reschedule and stop/start/offline transitions. A low-IPC classification
  must not delay completion or prevent the service needed to handle an interrupt.
- Contended locks, lock-holder preemption, acquire/release publication and bounded
  service for constrained LR/SC loops. Test compute+lock-holder and stream+waiter
  mixes. No kernel-PC, lock-address or register-specific policy; pause is a hint,
  not permission to lose wakeups or correctness.
- Context switches and SATP/ASID changes, SFENCE.VMA/shootdown IPIs, page faults,
  kernel/user transitions and stale translation responses. Policy statistics are
  per hardware hart, not automatically per Linux task; explicitly define whether
  they reset/decay across privilege/address-space changes. Investigate timing-
  interference/security implications rather than assuming history sharing is safe.
- CSR and atomic values/side effects, PMA/MMIO ordering, fences and DMA/coherent
  publication. Performance pressure never weakens permission or ordering checks.
- Lazy FP context and per-hart frm/fflags after Phase5; vector state, hypervisor and
  accelerator ownership have separate guards and are not implied by scalar FP.
- Kernel timer/RCU/watchdog/lockup observations are useful integration diagnostics,
  not ISA latency bounds. Separate legal external stalls, observer artifacts and
  actual starvation. Preserve original failure codes and structured trap records.

Progress through bare-metal controlled kernels -> supervisor SBI/IPI/timer/shootdown
litmus -> a pinned Linux boot/workload profile -> mixed kernel/user stress. The
current OpenSBI/HSM success covers only its recorded stage. QEMU can supply expected
software paths, not proof of RTL scheduling, cache RTT or memory-system coherence.
Keep Image/config/DTB/rootfs/tool identities when a Linux profile is established.

Evaluate static and adaptive candidates with identical binaries and service seeds,
role swaps, phase changes, cache-size/latency/backpressure sweeps, competing streams
and no-work/WFI controls. Check oscillation and recovery after abrupt phase changes;
repeat across declared seeds. Reject a candidate that violates a correctness/fairness
gate even if aggregate throughput improves. Report weighted speedup, worst-hart
slowdown, tail completion/interrupt service, miss traffic/pollution and area/state.
A generic-cell or simulation-wall-time reduction is not mapped area, power or IPC.
Only retain a policy when benefits exceed its control/port/fanout cost on the declared
workload envelope and static fallback remains independently validated.

##### Phase 7 — multicore coherence and cluster transaction lifetime

Use NC2/NH1 before NC2/NH2; preserve NC1 identity. g6lc_coherence hub tests must cover
held AR/AW payload and slot identity, competing admissions, W ownership, legal B
only after final W, RLAST/backpressure, mixed reads/writes, finite-response starvation
rotation and error/reset draining. Keep per-target invalidation obligations and
same-cycle queue pop/coalesce races. Prove a target really holds a cache line before
using an invalidation test as evidence; an uncached observer is a control, not proof.

Check hub versus inclusive-source arbitration, snoop-filter conflict conservatism,
L3 victim retention and L2 back-invalidation while fills/hits/stores race. Precise
snoop-filter displacement needs retained back-invalidation, not a sharer-bit shortcut.
Trace LR/SC/AMO reservation ownership through the actual core/cache/AXI path before
activating the currently tied-off hub LR inputs. Use cached/uncached, single/dual
worker and deliberate lost-notification controls. Do not infer this gate from NC1 SMT2.

##### Phase 8 — stream service, cache concurrency and storage efficiency

Preserve named depth-two MSHR savings in their proven serialized envelope. Before
raising depth, demonstrate multiple independent accepted and retained transactions
and useful overlap; a large MSHR array with peak occupancy one is not MLP. Separate
MSHR joins, waiter capacity, hit-under-miss, multiple fills, response/error association,
replay/cancel/fence and eviction/invalidation obligations. Demand and prefetch credits
must not starve a hart or lose response ownership.

Evaluate dependent pointer chains separately from independent streams; sweep working
sets around L1/L2/L3 sizes, shared/private lines, cacheability, stride, contention,
DRAM latency and legal backpressure. Observe actual prefetch acceptance, destination,
usefulness and pollution; counters/hints without a fill are not bandwidth benefit.
Tag SRAM/pipeline work needs explicit macro port schedule, reset/validity strategy,
fill/invalidate collision handling, DFT/scan and replay/latency contract. No silent
new demand-hit cycle. Synthesize the cluster and macro boundaries, not just cva6.

##### Phase 9 — interaction matrix, stability soak and promotion decision

Required boundary combinations: SMT handoff x trap/flush/miss; OoO recovery x WB/
commit/TID reuse; FP completion x cancellation/hart/class; predictor resolve x
checkpoint/bank ownership; stream traffic x demand/prefetch credits; coherence x
fills/evictions/inclusion; AMO/fence x all relevant memory queues. Start with directed
pairwise contracts and add simultaneous stress only after each mechanism is sound.
Test NC1/NH1, NC1/NH2, NC2/NH1, then explicitly supported NC2/NH2 variants; retain
minimum-feature identity and guard-negative configurations. Unsupported cells stay
refused, not marked skipped-as-pass.

Require repeatable architectural results, observer controls, corruption/mutation
negatives, reset/error recovery, fixed-budget soak, compliance, formal/cover scope,
full target elaboration and whole-cluster synthesis. Physical promotion additionally
needs approved libraries/macros, constraints, STA, activity-based power and DFT/CDC
review. The router1.25GHz target is inferred, not timing sign-off. Rank candidates
by useful throughput and worst-case latency at measured area/timing cost; publish
regressions as well as wins. Do not stack unrelated percentage gains or extrapolate
compiler runtime improvements into silicon IPC.

#### E. Progress ledger and immediate execution queue

- Phase0: current baseline/evidence reconciliation and rename elaboration are closed
  within scope. The collected broad gate has11 formal PASS tasks and one unresolved
  fetch-IQ failure (witness/cover interrupted), plus default lint8/54 and synthesis32/5
  warnings. Strict standalone slang remains skipped; no full green gate is claimed.
- Phase2 ownership increment: cancelled-WB and cancelled-retirement guards are
  directed-qualified. Final generic fixture86,824 cells versus88,501 before the
  retirement guard; state6,250 unchanged, zero latches/SCCs. No mapped PPA or IPC claim.
- Boot preservation: smt2-drop-regate-build-20260919 produced modelc421aedc...
  byte-for-byte. Subsequently opensbi-attribution-dual-v3-20260919 re-ran the same
  firmware on instrumented model2ce91d639b964cfb6067f23a4b510ab4d345624494a2211ba092f14f0f609837:
  strictDualPassed=true,12,765,628 cycles,333,635/8,932,406 retirements. Defaults are
  unchanged. The latest workload/analysis-only increment reuses that exact executable.
- Phases1 and3–9 remain open at their stated gates. Namespace and FP redesigns are
  anticipated, not implemented or promoted by the current component results.

1. Recover/check the fetch-IQ witness and complete remaining verification gaps;
   preserve the completed WB/LSQ/rename/drop controls as regression prerequisites.
2. Extend Phase2 into full-core commit/exception and late-response lifetime cases;
   do not re-label the closed cancelled-retirement fixture as full architectural closure.
3. Extend the now-measured symmetric dual-runnable/solo baseline with asymmetric
   cache, dependency and lock/IPI phases, readiness/switch-reason and RTT attribution
   (Phase1); do not retune the passing scheduler or modify the pinned firmware.
4. Design the actual late-result PRF bridge and legal enabled-OoO baseline with
   explicit port/ordering trade-offs (Phases2–3); no hart/FP guard removal yet.
5. Advance later phases only at their dependency exits; keep separate commits/change
   sets for owner correctness, scheduling policy, predictor, cache storage and MLP.

Every implementation increment records its current phase, precise exit criteria,
source tuple, before/after/control results, config/ISA/DTS impact, DFT/reset/clock
impact, new critical cones and area/state budget in the existing AGENTS maps,
design documentation and relevant guide. Update this plan rather than creating
competing roadmaps. Stage large namespace/FP changes at explicit architecture-review
points; do not turn a boot PASS into an omnibus feature-enable change.

### Historical reassessment — 2026-09-16

**This section supersedes the original RR-first work order and obsolete conclusions
in the historical archive below.** The objective is faster single-core useful work
and scalable multicore throughput per area, with stability and preservation of the
validated dual-hart SMT2 integer pass as hard constraints. This is not a plan to
maximize nominal issue width, cache size or advertised GHz.

Review basis: current worktree at HEAD `78c40fb86`, plus immutable source/model/ELF
records from the preceding optimization passes. The latest HEAD change is BIOS
work, not a change to the promoted core paths. Concurrent BIOS/APU/QEMU/AI changes
are not owned by this task. This reassessment inspects code and existing evidence;
it does not claim fresh simulations or new measured gains.

#### 1. Scoped promotion is complete; release qualification is not

The following optimizations are already in the working RTL, not merely in isolated
experiment trees. Current file hashes match the retained, validated versions.
No additional behavior change is needed to promote these same edits again.

| Retained change | Established benefit | Important limit |
|---|---|---|
| `core/fetch_B/instr_queue.sv`: circular selection after compact insertion; head advances by actual prefix fires | Matched four-slot/I2/H2 leaf: 22,354 → 20,085 generic cells, 5,954 → 5,426 sequential cells; -10.15% generic cells and -528 state bits | Not whole-core or physical area. Earlier compaction's 25,618 → 22,354 is a separate comparison; do not add percentages |
| `core/fetch_B/frontend.sv`: asynchronous predictor PC follows the transaction being realigned | Correct lookup association, with existing FPGA selector phase preserved | PC alignment alone did not deliver the measured throughput win; PH_BHT's separately wired registered path is unchanged |
| `core/frontend/g6lc_bp_statcor.sv`: absolute outcome counter is consumed as an absolute bias, not an inversion flag | Combined with lookup alignment: 1/8 KiB checked locality 213,009 → 147,470 cycles; 64 KiB 213,006 → 147,467; about -30.77%. Hot-scan 275,593 → 262,740, -4.66% | Corrector leaf costs 1,261 → 1,360 generic cells (+99), with 192 state bits unchanged. Cost applies to the enabled corrector; no aggregate mapped-area or power claim |
| Live-IQ formal storage invariants and binary SAT route | Explicit base plus two-step induction; all 12 covers within 28 steps, including no-flush full drain; positive/negative controls | Proof-only change. Four depth-eight banks, two targets, two harts/issues, FW64/RVC, XLEN/VLEN/GPLEN32, ASIC/non-RVH; not all widths, FPGA, X behavior or full SMT |

Retained RTL identities:
- IQ: `7fba053f6744f4c907afe1e32a613b3832297edf8113c0fcd0f4c4402a3cc645`.
- Frontend: `626dfa089730ba0f2fe7ced73d8bc811ab24de83897461fa08cf7639e90cee39`.
- Corrector: `6064905a249a71dbed7abdf66d4a525c61eb39eadb391dee5767d82052990651`.
- SMT2 package after depth-two promotion: `b61109d7c17d00520f73ef07c49d8aa9aa751372e651e200500b4e4e5aed39f4`.
- Stream8 package after depth-two promotion: `f2c564c9d4fd9af0e2178c8273620772a5035860821c6361050e3a7ce1781bc3`.
- Earlier depth16/auto8 package hashes remain in the immutable baseline manifests.

Evidence anchors: `architecture/core-fetch/README.md`,
`architecture/l2-l3-cache/README.md`, and artifacts
`review-predictor-promotion-20260916`, `review-statcor-retained-20260916`,
`review-predictor-smt2-20260916`, `review-predictor-work-20260916`,
`review-predictor-synth-20260916`, `review-predictor-pc-proof-20260916`,
`review-iq-sat-binary-20260916`, `review-iq-cover-binary-20260916` and their
negative controls. Do not recompute frozen measurements merely to reformat them.

**SMT2 preservation baseline:** all 13 execution records passed; independent RVI
and mixed C/I references checked 13,699/14,203 retirements, 25,774 operands and
1,025 known loads each, with no skipped checks. One peer-flag value remains outside
the memory-value oracle. Both 48 KiB/hart encodings, redirect-chain, observer
controls and injected-error checks passed. Retired tail/spin counts may change
with timing; instruction/operand correctness, not byte-identical timed traces
across different RTL, is the cross-candidate gate.

Minimal 32/64-bit, SMT2 and stream8 lint/strict elaboration passed. SMT2 and stream8
synthesis smoke reported zero check problems. The named licensing diagnostic is
not registered; manual tier/notice review and the recorded tier-R authorization
are retained, not described as an automated legal PASS.

**Not promoted:** executable-region cacheability exemption removal, WT allocation
attributes, RR default-on, scheduler retuning, nonblocking L2, wider issue/retirement,
new prefetch policy, new clock/voltage targets or a general SMT2 SKU. No multicore
scaling speedup has been established by the locality/hot-scan results. A two-core
package with one active measured worker is not a two-worker throughput result.

#### Continuation: bounded supply measurement and invalidation-leaf repair

The follow-up now has new evidence, separate from the initial read-only reassessment:

- `review-fetch-supply-analysis-20260916` analyzes immutable observer data from the
  retained predictor model. Interval [5182,29986) contains 24,804 complete clock
  rows and 1,378 loop intervals: 12,402 requests, responses, IQ transfers, decoded
  allocations and committed retirements. Requests are offered every cycle but
  accepted every two; accepted-request-to-response latency is one cycle. There
  are no I-cache misses, memory requests, mispredictions or architectural flushes
  in that interval. Dropped-row/wrong-PC controls are rejected. This is bounded
  core-0 analysis, not a new full-ROI or scaling result. IQ-empty/readiness and
  backend stall reasons remain unobserved; do not infer them from missing transfers.
- `review-inval-before-20260916` reproduces both leaf defects against a passing
  control: global ready wrongly overrides a full noncoalescing target, and a
  same-line request merged into a departing sole entry is lost.
- The retained leaf candidate (`g6lc_inval_bus.sv`, `2cafee7f…`) shares per-target
  eligibility between admission and update. Every target must have room or a
  retainable tail; a departing sole entry cannot absorb a new command. No new
  state/stage/clock/port/config is added. Full nonmerging queues deliberately do
  not borrow simultaneous-pop credit; depth-one producers retry after drain.
- `review-inval-after-20260916` passes N/depth 1/1, 2/1, 3/2, 4/3, 4/4, each with

#### Implemented: held AR/AW owner and slot reservations

The hub candidate now fixes the four address/ID/credit failures. It retains only

acceptance. A held offer may consume its reserved slot even when the pool is full.
A new AW gives up the first free slot only when a new AR actually competes.

No full AXI payload buffer or new pipeline stage is added. Legal AXI masters must
hold their valid/payload until ready; this precondition is explicit in the formal
harness. Source widths, configuration, queue depth, clock and reset strategy are
unchanged. Pending selections override RR/starvation selection; existing force
events are suppressed while the held selection wins, without adding PMU selectors.
