#### 2026-09-15 — stream8 zero-delta root cause: execute-region load quirk

Whole-kernel PMU on `mini_l2_hot_scan` (mcycle+hpm3..8 to DRAM `report`, read via `log mem` rules; `g6lc_tb` never services HTIF syscalls — kernel cannot print):

- rr0 `hot-scan-pmu-rr0f`: cyc=275,593 l2m=4 l1m=0 lds=20,483 dac=20,492 i1m=17 sts=7; kernel PASS tohost=1.
- ROI-only run earlier: cyc=213,429, l2m=2, l1m=0, lds=16,387, dac=16,396.

`l1m=0` across ~544 KiB of unique data through a 32 KiB D$ is impossible for allocating loads. Root cause: `cva6_hpdcache_if_adapter.sv:94-96` forces a load **uncacheable** when its paddr sits inside an *execute* region (S4/2jr workaround), and every shipped config maps `ExecuteRegion[all-DRAM] = 0x8000_0000+0x4000_0000` ⊇ the whole cached region. On every HPDCACHE target, all DRAM data loads are uncached: L1 never allocates, `hpdcache_mem_to_axi_read.sv:59-63` emits `ar.cache=0010`, `l2_is_cacheable` fails, L2 sees only I$ refills (i1m=17 ≈ l2m=4+hits). Identical rr0/rr1 core cycles are therefore expected — L2 replacement never sees data traffic. This is the "high cache miss" finding: effectively 100% miss on DRAM data (~13.4 cyc/load), and the L2 is an I$ sidecar on stream8.

Store port has no execute check (stores stay cacheable) but the L2 write path bypasses, so stores never populate L2 either.

Diagnostic in flight: overlay knob `ExecuteRegionDramLen` (shrink-only rewrite of the first-listed `ExecuteRegionLength` element) + `;`-separated `SOFT_LADDER_OVERLAY` fields → `iso-stream8-e8k-rr{0,1}` with exec DRAM region = 32 KiB so the hot-scan buffer is cached-non-execute. Then loads allocate, L2 sees data traffic, the same kernel discriminates RR, and the delta vs the uncached run quantifies the quirk cost. Whether the quirk can be narrowed without reintroducing the 2jr hang is a product decision — not silently changed.

#### 2026-09-15 (cont.) — e8k overlay: first real RR discrimination at core level

`iso-stream8-e8k-rr{0,1}` = stream8 + `L2RoundRobinEn={0,1}` + `ExecuteRegionDramLen=8000` (DRAM exec rule shrunk to 32 KiB so `0x80008000+` buffer is cached-non-execute). Same tree, same kernel (`mini_l2_hot_scan_pmu`), whole-kernel PMU report via `log mem`:

| counter | rr0 (`s8-e8k-rr0-pmu`) | rr1 (`s8-e8k-rr1-pmu`) |
|---|---|---|
| cyc | 374,177 | **325,980 (−12.9%)** |
| l2m | 18,787 | **13,318 (−29.1%)** |
| l1m | 18,857 | 18,857 |
| lds / dac / sts / i1m | 20,483 / 20,524 / 7 / 80 | identical |

- Overlay mechanism confirmed: `ExecuteRegionDramLen` rewrites the first-listed `ExecuteRegionLength` element (the all-DRAM rule); `SOFT_LADDER_OVERLAY` separator is now `,` (`;` broke the remote shell).
- RR keeps the hot line resident under the all-set scan where way-0 victim evicts it: +5,469 L2 hits ≈ 8.7 cyc/avoided-miss. Workload is deliberately RR-favorable — evidence a win *exists*, not promotion. `protected_hot`-style patterns can still favor baseline.
- Interpretation guard: this measures the L2 policy on real cacheable traffic; the earlier "0 delta" runs were the uncacheable quirk, now superseded.

#### 2026-09-15 (cont.) — SMT2 livelock/convergence resolution

- `iso-smt2-rr1` livelock (800k cy, IQ ghost-head freeze) ran on the **pre-fix build** (exe 06:21 < fix commit 08:30). Not RR-attributable: smt2 WT emits `AxCACHE=0010` on all channels → `l2_is_cacheable` fails → L2 replacement sees no traffic.
- Current-tree `iso-smt2-rr0` (vthreads=12) hit a **deterministic** `Active region did not converge` at t=287 (pmp LZC retriggers; `dec_pc`/`dec_ex` re-evaluating in-step). Bisect: reverting only the drain tweak in `g6lc_l2_top.sv` (commit `035c65c89`) → boots, kernel PASS 22,964 cy. Re-adding it + `SOFT_LADDER_VERILATOR_THREADS=1` (`iso-smt2-vt1`) → boots, PASS 22,964 cy.
- Conclusion: **no true comb loop** — the drain fix is value-neutral on this traffic (smt2 `slv_r_ready = ~invalidate` ≈ 1; `invalidate` only pulses on atomic grants, none in the kernel). The nonconvergence is a **Verilator 5.008 threaded-scheduler artifact** of the vthreads=12 netlist when the new comb edge `mst_r_ready ← slv_r_ready` lands. Workaround for smt2 verlibs: `SOFT_LADDER_VERILATOR_THREADS=1`. RTL stays — it is AXI-correct (ready may depend on valid; valid never on ready downstream).
- Open: rerun `iso-smt2-rr1` rebuilt from the current tree to confirm the livelock does not return (then the old livelock = pre-fix beat-drop or a different fetch-side latent bug; both possibilities still need the fixed-tree data point).

#### 2026-09-15 (cont.2) — SMT2 "RR livelock" resolved: Verilator vthreads artifact, not RTL

Full 2×2 (same current tree, same ELF `mini_checked_work_n1_8k`):

| verlib | vthreads | RR | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `iso-smt2-rr0` | 12 | 0 | `Active region did not converge` @ t=287 (deterministic ×2) |
| `iso-smt2-rr0` (drain revert, bisect) | 12 | 0 | PASS 22,964 |
| `iso-smt2-vt1` | 1 | 0 | PASS 22,964 |
| `iso-smt2-rr1-vt1` | 1 | 1 | **PASS 22,964** |
| `iso-smt2-rr1` (rebuilt current) | 12 | 1 | livelock 800k (×2, same PC window) |
| `iso-smt2-rr1` @06:21 (pre-fix) | 12 | 1 | livelock 800k |

- smt2 `AxCACHE=0010` on every channel ⇒ L2 sees only bypass ⇒ RR netlist is **functionally dead** on smt2; rr0/rr1 can only diverge through simulator artifacts. `--x-initial 0` + StallRandom*=0 ⇒ deterministic init/timing.
- vt1 rr0/rr1 **both pass identically at 22,964 cy** ⇒ current tree is functionally clean; the earlier livelock and the t=287 nonconvergence are the **same Verilator 5.008 MTask artifact** expressed differently per partition. The drain tweak only perturbs partitioning (it is value-neutral here: `slv_r_ready = ~invalidate ≈ 1`; `invalidate` pulses only on atomic grants, and the kernel uses no atomics).
- Correction to earlier guidance: **"RR livelocks SMT2" is retracted.** RR is moot on smt2 (no cacheable traffic). The real risk is vthreads=12 model non-bit-exactness on this netlist — smt2 qualification/soak runs should use `SOFT_LADDER_VERILATOR_THREADS=1` (or a fixed partition). The pre-fix drain bug (forced `r_ready=1` in `S_BYPASS_R` could drop beats if a slave ever backpressured) was still a real defect worth the fix — just not the livelock cause.
- Durable note needed: record the vthreads artifact + the vt1 smt2 rule in `build-platform/AGENTS.md` and the multi-threading dev logs.

## Part B — architecture/out-of-order/README.md history (through 2026-09-21)

### Historical investigation: exception redirect and offset assertion (2026-09-20)

**Status: reproduced, bounded, owner located, mechanism NOT yet identified. No repair
attempted.** This supersedes the earlier reading of the firmware stop as "a later
`addr_offset0` assertion"; that assertion was the instrument, and repairing it exposed
this.

**Oracle repair first (H3).** `load_unit.sv`'s `addr_offset0`/`addr_offset1` assertions
fired on an architecturally **legal** misaligned load. A misaligned access is a legal
program event: the LSU raises `LD_ADDR_MISALIGNED` and the request is killed in
SEND_TAG, but the D$ request is already granted because the exception arrives a cycle
after the index is applied — so the offset legitimately reaches the load buffer. The
upstream comment said the condition "actually triggers a load address misaligned
exception" while the severity was `$fatal`; `git show HEAD` confirms both are upstream
and unmodified. Directed stage32 reproduces the abort in **796 cycles on OoO and1582
on in-order** (`ooo-offset-{ooo,inorder}-s32-before-v1`), so the oracle was invalid in
both execution modes and was **not** an OoO signal. The antecedent is now qualified by
the captured offset's own alignment, which keeps the real content — that the recorded
offset agrees with the access width — and keeps `$fatal` for that genuine inconsistency.
Nothing was demoted to a warning and no misaligned case is exempted from its exception.

**What the repair exposed.** Two frozen ELFs of the same source, differing only in
branch immediates, on the **same** model `f0344d76...`, replayed twice each:

| frozen ELF | after `LD_ADDR_MISALIGNED` @ `0x800001f8` | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `ooo-offset-ooo-s32-phase-v1` | → `0x80000210` = `mtvec`; `mcause=4`, `mepc=0x800001f8`; `mret` → `0x800001fc` | PASS907 |
| `ooo-offset-ooo-s32-after-v2` | → **`0x80000206`**, skipping `0x800001fc/1fe/0202`; handler never entered | FAIL (tohost=1) |

Both are deterministic across replays, so this is **not** non-determinism (H4 satisfied).
RVFI and `[commit-dbg]` agree the exception is raised with `cause=4` at the correct PC in
both. The violated promise is therefore **commit → frontend redirect**: the trap is
reported but the restart PC is not `mtvec`. `0x80000206` is the sequential PC following
the co-issued group that contains the faulting instruction, which is consistent with the
trap redirect losing to a competing window/sequential advance — but that is a hypothesis,
not a measurement, and no RTL change is made on it.

**Why the firmware saw this.** The witness run `ooo-osbi-offset-witness-v1` (enriched
`$fatal` message) recorded `va=0x2f offset=7 tid=0 commit_tid=4 ex=0 cancelled=0
state=SEND_TAG`. The load is **younger than the commit head**, so it may be an unresolved
wrong-path access, and `speculative=0` is uninformative because this config sets
`SpeculativeSb=0`. `NEGATIVE.md` records that inferring a defect from a *small non-zero
address* was tried and reverted five times (I4bv → I4ca → G0 → G1i → W1), so that
inference is explicitly not made here.

**Partition ANSWERED: this is not an OoO defect.** A one-axis control was built with
`verif/regress/isolated-config-overlay.py` extended to allowlist `OoOEn`, so both arms come
from one recorded source state rather than from an edited package — `OoOEn: 1 → 0` on
`g6lc64_ooo_int`, same single hart, same two issue ports, everything else untouched
(model `e02f1f2d29b711a50ca0c3cd95db569ad11de271aea58c6b76958b324bc53444`, overlay
`fields={'OoOEn': {'from': '1', 'to': '0'}}`). Replaying the same two frozen ELFs:

| frozen ELF | OoO `f0344d76…` | in-order control `e02f1f2d…` |
|---|---|---|
| `…-s32-after-v2` | FAIL, redirect to `0x80000206` | **timeout**: `cause=4` raised, then NO further retirement |
| `…-s32-phase-v1` | PASS907 | **PASS925** |

The failure reproduces with `OoOEn=0`, so the two-issue OoO path is **exonerated** for this
finding. The symptom differs by backend — in-order stops retiring after the exception while
OoO restarts at the wrong PC — which is consistent with a shared trap-restart defect whose
downstream expression depends on the backend, not with an OoO-specific one. The in-order arm
again printed `*** SUCCESS *** (tohost = 0) after 20000 cycles` at the cycle cap and the
runner correctly classified it `outcome: timeout` (the H3 guard doing its job).

Consequence for scope: this belongs to the shared exception/restart path, not to the OoO
increment, and it must not be counted against or fixed inside the OoO work.

**Mechanism located (not yet explained).** `+smt_flow_trace` on the failing arm, needing no
rebuild, shows the restart source directly:

```
cycle=781 alloc id=15 pc=0x800001f8 (lw)   alloc id=16 pc=0x800001fc   same 2-issue group
cycle=785 wb    id=15 pc=0x800001f8 ex=1                               the load faults
t=798     [commit-dbg] pc=0x800001f8 ex.valid=1 cause=4                the trap commits
cycle=789 alloc id=0 gen=11 pc=0x80000206                              frontend restarts HERE
cycle=792 retire       pc=0x80000206
```

`0x800001fe` and `0x80000202` are never allocated: the frontend resumes the **sequential
stream** at `0x80000206` instead of the `mtvec` value `0x80000210`, even though `controller.sv`
clears `set_pc_commit_o` on `ex_valid_i` precisely so PC-gen takes the commit-supplied trap
vector. The surviving owner is therefore the PC-gen priority between the commit exception PC
and a frontend-internal redirect — which `controller.sv` already flags in prose: *"Matching
taken Jump must not flush_if: without NPC reseed it discards the correct target stream (early
load-misalign/illegal)"*.

**Candidate co-factor REFUTED, not weakened.** The prediction was that the failing ELF's two
consumer branches sharing one target (`0x8000025e`) was the co-factor, the passing ELF having
distinct targets. Stage34 is stage32 with exactly that one change — both branches to a single
label — with the load forced to an 8-byte boundary so the geometry is reproduced byte for byte:

```
800001f8: lw   a5,6(sp)                    the faulting 4-byte load
800001fc: li   t0,1                        2-byte co-issued op
800001fe: bne  a4,t0,8000027e <fail_alias>  4-byte, straddles the window boundary
80000202: li   t0,85
80000206: bne  a5,t0,8000027e <fail_alias>  same shared target
```

It **passes on both models**, and it is not vacuous: the trace shows
`LD_ADDR_MISALIGNED @ 0x800001f8` followed immediately by `0x80000210`, the correct `mtvec`.
Per T3 the hypothesis is therefore replaced rather than softened: **shared branch targets are
not the co-factor.** What remains is narrower and currently unexplained — the same geometry
with the shared target at `0x8000025e` fails while at `0x8000027e` it passes. Both targets sit
at offset6 of their 8-byte window and are 2-byte aligned, so the obvious structural
properties are already equal; no supported candidate remains.

Stage34 is kept as a passing regression because it pins this geometry: a future change that
breaks misaligned-trap redirect for the co-issued/straddling shape will now fail a seconds-long
directed test rather than a firmware soak. The frozen ELF pair stays the only reproducer of the
failure itself.

**Mechanism identified: instruction supply survives the exception flush (P3 channel3).** The
improved observation compares the same cycle window in both arms:

| event | failing ELF | passing ELF |
|---|---|---|
| faulting load writes back | `cycle=785 wb id=15 pc=0x800001f8 ex=1` | — |
| exception flush | `cycle=786 flush=1 flush_unissued=1 resolve=0` | `cycle=789 flush=1 flush_unissued=1 resolve=0` |
| first post-flush allocation | `cycle=789 gen=11 pc=0x80000206` — **3 cycles later** | `cycle=797 gen=11 pc=0x80000210` = `mtvec` — **8 cycles later** |
| consequence | `cycle=791 resolve=1 mispredict=1 target=0x8000025e` → `fail` | clean handler entry |

So the commit-side flush is **correct in both arms**; `controller.sv` asserts it and clears
`set_pc_commit_o` as intended. What differs is what the frontend then supplies. Eight cycles is
the cost of a genuine redirect plus an I$ access, which the passing arm pays; three cycles is
too few for a refetch, so the failing arm is serving an instruction that was **already buffered**
when the flush asserted. The wrongly-supplied branch then executes and resolves as a mispredict
to `0x8000025e`, which is the `fail` exit — the observed verdict is a *consequence*, not the fault.

**CORRECTION to the reading above (T9, walked back the same session).** The latency gap invited
the conclusion "pre-flush instruction supply survived the kill", and that was recorded before it
was checked. Reading `instr_queue.sv` refutes the specific mechanism: `flush_i` is wired to both
FIFO instances and the same condition resets `idx_ds_q`/`idx_is_q`/`push_seq_q`, so the
instruction queue **is** cleared on flush. A surviving queue entry is therefore not an available
explanation, and the channel-3 attribution is withdrawn to a hypothesis.

What survives as **observed fact** is narrower, and it is what the record should carry:

- the commit-side exception flush asserts correctly in both arms;
- the first post-flush allocation in the failing arm is a **pre-flush-stream PC**
  (`0x80000206`, sequential after the faulting instruction) rather than `mtvec`;
- it arrives in3 cycles versus8 for the correct redirect, so the two arms differ in the
  *provenance* of the delivered instruction, not only in a computed address;
- the later mispredict to `0x8000025e` and the `tohost=1` verdict are both downstream of that.

What is **not** distinguished: whether the sequential PC was delivered from some frontend
structure that the flush does not reach (loop buffer, FTQ, an I$ output register, or a realigner
carry — none of which has been inspected), or whether PC-gen itself selected the sequential PC.
Those remain two live candidates with no evidence separating them, so no owner is named.

Still **no repair attempted**, and now explicitly no mechanism claim either.

**Frontend inspection done; three candidates eliminated.** Every structure that can hold a
fetched instruction or PC across `flush_if` was checked against the flush in `frontend.sv`:
`instr_queue` (`.flush_i(flush_i)`), the realigner leftover (`.flush_i` plus
`leftover_kill = is_mispredict | flush_i | …`), the FTQ and loop buffer
(`.flush_i(flush_i | is_mispredict | bp_fire)`), the predictor
(`flush_i | is_mispredict | flush_bp_i`), `icache_valid_q` (cleared on
`flush_i || is_mispredict || bp_fire`) and `inflight_q` (cleared on `kill_s2`). All are
flush-qualified.

The one structure that is **not** flush-qualified is `replay_q`/`replay_addr_q`
(`frontend.sv:170-178`, no `flush_i` term), and `replay_q` outranks both the sequential step and
the prediction in the `npc_select` chain — a real latent asymmetry worth recording. It is
**not** this defect: `+fetch_win_trace` shows `rp=0` at the exception flush in the failing arm.

The decisive negative: the `[win]` line at the exception flush is **identical in both arms** —
`vaddr=0x80000208 k1=1 k2=1 bpf=0 fl=1 mp=0 rp=0 npc=0x80000218` (failing `t=799`, passing
`t=802`). Same in-flight fetch, same kills, same flush, same sequentially-selected `npc`, and
`arch_valid` low in both since `npc` took the `if_ready` branch. So "the trap vector was not
selected at the flush cycle" is true of the **passing** arm too and explains nothing.

Eliminated, none weakened: (1) shared branch targets, (2) a queue entry surviving the kill,
(3) `replay_q` survival and `arch_valid`-at-flush as discriminators.

**Divergence isolated to one cycle after the flush: a killed I$ response is accepted.** The
cycle-by-cycle `[win]` diff over the interval the flush cycle could not explain:

```
passing  t=799 rsp=1 vaddr=80000200 take=1 ... una=1 vmask=0011
         t=802 rsp=0 vaddr=80000208 take=0 k1=1 k2=1 fl=1 vmask=0000 npc=80000218   flush
         t=808 rsp=1 vaddr=80000210 take=1 k1=0 k2=0 fl=0 vmask=0011                mtvec fetched
         t=812 rsp=1 vaddr=80000218 take=1

failing  t=797 rsp=1 vaddr=80000200 take=1 ... una=1 vmask=0011
         t=799 rsp=0 vaddr=80000208 take=0 k1=1 k2=1 fl=1 vmask=0000 npc=80000218   flush, identical
         t=800 rsp=1 vaddr=80000208 take=1 k1=0 k2=1 bpf=1 fl=0 una=1 vmask=0111 npc=80000248
         t=804 rsp=0 vaddr=80000246 take=0 k1=1 k2=1 fl=1 mp=1 npc=80000260
         t=812 rsp=1 vaddr=8000025e take=1                                          the fail exit
```

At `t=800` the I$ response for `0x80000208` — **the same window whose request was killed at
`t=799` with `k1=1 k2=1`** — returns and is **accepted** (`take=1`), pushing three instructions
(`vmask=0111`, `una=1`) and firing a prediction (`bpf=1`) that redirects `npc` to `0x80000248`.
The passing arm accepts nothing until `t=808`, and what it accepts is `0x80000210`, the trap
vector. So the wrongly-accepted window is **upstream of everything else**: it supplies the
pre-flush-stream instructions, it moves `npc` away from the trap vector, and the `t=804`
mispredict to the `fail` exit is its consequence.

The promise violated is therefore at the **I$ response-acceptance boundary**: a killed request's
response must not be accepted after the kill. `kill_s1`/`kill_s2` are asserted for the flush
cycle only, and `inflight_q` is cleared by `kill_s2` in that same cycle, so a response arriving
on the following cycle meets a frontend that no longer tracks it and an `icache_take` that is no
longer inhibited (`fl=0` by then). This is visibility channel3 again — state surviving a kill —
but at the response boundary, **not** in the instruction queue, whose flush qualification was
already verified. Layout sensitivity follows: whether the response lands on the kill cycle or the
one after depends on I$ hit/miss timing and the fetch sequence, which branch displacements
perturb (P2).

**Confirmed by a dedicated instrument.** `+fetch_kill_check` (`frontend.sv`, `translate_off`,
report-only) tracks "an outstanding fetch was killed and has not been replaced" — a genuine
redirect clears it by issuing a new request, so only a stale response being taken can report. On
the matched pair:

```
failing   [killchk] t=800 take=1 rsp_vaddr=80000208 killed_vaddr=80000208 same=1 npc=80000248
passing   (silent for the whole run)
```

One report, on the failing arm only, at exactly the divergence cycle, with `same=1` proving the
accepted response belongs to the **same address that was killed**. The passing arm never takes a
killed response once in2000 cycles, so this is not a frequent benign event that happens to
coincide with the failure. Together with the `[win]` diff — identical at the flush, divergent on
the next cycle — the causal chain from "killed response accepted" to "trap vector never fetched"
to "mispredict into the `fail` exit" is now closed by observation.

**Repair implemented: kill persistence.** `frontend.sv` now remembers a killed in-flight fetch
(`kill_owed_q`/`kill_owed_addr_q`) until either its response is seen or a replacement fetch is
issued, and `icache_take` is gated by `kill_drop` — a response is dropped only when its address
matches the killed one. The address match is what prevents dropping a legitimate return: the state
is live only in the window between the kill and the next request, and it compares **addresses, not
fetched data**, so no control decision is made from a data value (channel5 stays closed). No new
clock or reset, `always_ff`/`always_comb` separation preserved, build clean at the baseline 9
warnings with none added.

Before/after on the matched frozen pair, same model:

| frozen ELF | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `…-s32-after-v2` | FAIL — `take=1` on the killed window, `npc=0x80000248` | **PASS906** — `take=0 drop=1`, `npc=0x80000218` |
| `…-s32-phase-v1` | PASS907 | **PASS907**, checker silent |

The probe now reports `take=0 drop=1 same=1` exactly once at `t=800`, which is the repair acting at
the identified cycle rather than a behaviour change elsewhere.

Regression on the repaired model: stages17,20,22,25,29,30,31,32,33,34 pass Spike-matched
(918/913/936/959/896/894/906/906/927/902 cycles) and stage26 passes self-checking at911 — it is
Spike-compared only by mistake, since its LR/store/SC sequence is unconstrained and both outcomes
are legal. Cycle counts sit within about ±3 of the pre-repair runs, consistent with one dropped
stale fetch.

**REGRESSION AGAINST THE PROTECTED ANCHOR — the repair is NOT qualified and must not be adopted
as it stands.** Rebuilt in-order SMT2 model `3a1e97422efbc8c3…`, same firmware and profile as the
anchor. The boot reached **12,731,487 cycles** — just short of the **12,765,628** it previously
completed — and stopped on a different assertion:

```
%Error: pmp_entry.sv:81: Assertion failed in …i_cva6_mmu.i_ptw.i_pmp_ptw.gen_pmp.genblk1[1].i_pmp_entry
outcome: error   strictDualPassed: false   timedOut: false
```

`pmp_entry.sv:78-86` checks that a NAPOT `pmpaddr` is well formed (`conf_addr_i[size-3] == 0` and
all lower bits ones). It is an upstream `translate_off` check on **PMP CSR contents**, so it is
sensitive to observing a `pmpaddr` mid-update.

**RETRACTED — the control below was CONFOUNDED and does not exonerate the frontend.** Every arm in
the table still carried an unaccounted `load_unit.sv` delta: the enriched `$fatal` diagnostic added
for the witness run, never removed. So no arm was the zero-delta test it was presented as. With
`load_unit.sv` restored to the passing build's content byte-for-byte (hash `d875f999…`, verified
before spending simulation time) and the repair enabled, the anchor **still** stops at
`pmp_entry.sv:81` at 12,731,487 (model `70da7cac…`). The source now differs from the passing build in
only two files — `core/fetch_B/frontend.sv` and `corev_apu/tb/g6lc_tb.cpp`.

The true single-delta test has since been run (model `145c8d74…`): `load_unit.sv` byte-identical to
the passing build **and** `G6LC_NO_KILL_PERSIST`, so the frontend elaborates to the passing design.
The anchor **still** stops at `pmp_entry.sv:81` at 12,731,487. **The kill-persistence repair is
therefore exonerated on a clean arm** — which happens to agree with the earlier claim, but that
earlier claim was confounded and its agreement was luck, not evidence.

Five session builds now stop at exactly 12,731,487 while the single pre-session build passes at
12,765,628, on identical firmware, profile, DTB, bootrom, plusargs and cycle cap (706/712 recorded
keys match). What remains is the `g6lc_tb.cpp` allowlist string and `frontend.sv`'s inert residual —
and beyond those, the builder itself. If reverting both still fails, this anchor cannot be used as a
gate until the build environment is understood. The lesson is specific: delta accounting that is not
hash-verified against the passing build is not accounting.

Historical, confounded control — a `G6LC_NO_KILL_PERSIST` seam widened until the disabled arm was
believed to carry a zero frontend delta:

| arm | model | anchor result |
|---|---|---|
| old build, pre-dating this work | `work-ver-smt2-loadcancel-v2` | **PASS 12,765,628** |
| kill persistence ON | `3a1e97422efbc8c3…` | `pmp_entry.sv:81` at **12,731,487** |
| drop disabled, state+probe kept | `64fcafecf9aabc73…` | `pmp_entry.sv:81` at **12,731,487** |
| zero frontend delta | `c196c15dd04275cc…` | `pmp_entry.sv:81` at **12,731,487** |

Same assertion, same instance, **same cycle**, across three different builds. The repair, its state
and its probe are all excluded, and the first reading of this as "the repair regressed the anchor" is
withdrawn. A wall-time asymmetry (1224s versus761s) was also discarded rather than used: the
synthesis gate was running concurrently on the same builder, so duration is not evidence here. The
directed evidence for the repair stands unaffected (the failing frozen ELF passes, the probe inverts
to `take=0 drop=1` at the identified cycle, eleven stages pass).

What remains is a **separate, open anchor regression**: this revision no longer completes the boot
that completed at12,765,628 cycles earlier in the session, and the cause is upstream of the frontend
work. Candidates not yet distinguished: an over-strict upstream NAPOT check firing on a transient
mid-update `pmpaddr` (OpenSBI writes `pmpaddr` and `pmpcfg` in separate instructions, so a malformed
pair is observable between them), versus a real wrong value. The tempting inference — "another
over-strict upstream assertion, like the one this session opened with" — is exactly what must be
checked rather than assumed.

Note the bisection this hands over: the only behavioural RTL delta since the last passing anchor is
the frontend repair, and the control has just excluded it. So the exposure lies in one of the
non-behavioural changes (the `load_unit` assertion antecedents, which are `translate_off`, or the
harness/test/tooling edits) or the anchor was already latent at that revision. That is the next
thing to settle, and it is **not** part of the OoO increment.

`+fetch_kill_check` stays report-only. Full qualification of the repair — anchor, guarded dual-hart
set, FP suite, lint, full-core synthesis — is still outstanding and now blocked behind the anchor
question rather than behind the repair.

It is deliberately **not** part of the OoO increment: the finding reproduces at `OoOEn=0`, so it
belongs to the shared frontend.

The instrument is registered in the harness plusarg allowlist (`corev_apu/tb/g6lc_tb.cpp`) — an
unlisted plusarg is handed to HTIF and kills the run before the probe arms, which is how the first
attempt failed and is why the allowlist entry is part of the artifact rather than an afterthought.

**No repair attempted, deliberately.** PC-gen redirect priority is the area with the heaviest
recorded negative history (`NEGATIVE.md`: TAGE RAS restore → `IAF mepc=0x1400000000`, reseed →
`PC=0x4`, and the ~1,600-predicate `g1*` soup). Reordering that priority on a single
observation is exactly the move that record forbids; the co-factor mini is the cheaper and
falsifiable next step.

**Durable artifacts (T10).** `ooo_mem_min.S` stages32/33 with one exit code per phase
(cause4, tval5, mepc6, trap-count7, destination-written8), registered as

proves a **wrong-path** misaligned load is architecturally invisible: it passes at915

### Current reanalysis: cancellation before cache grant (2026-09-20)

This section supersedes conflicting interpretations in the historical increments
below; their measured results remain retained. The coding philosophy, complete
SMT2 reasoning trio, runtime-learning guide and full active/historical plan were
re-read against the current fetch_B-only boundary. The useful transfer from
fetch_A/smt_legacy is contract-local diagnosis and negative controls, not restoring
retired predicates or using a cookie as an architectural oracle.

**Observed mechanism.** The frozen experimental firmware/model pair
`6b2bad99...` / `09b03a0b...` fails before cycle 118,744. A 200,000-cycle capture
(`ooo-osbi-firstfault-review-v1`) reproduces the same 42,592 hart-1 retirements and
parked endpoint as the earlier 16M run. `ooo-osbi-observer-prefix-v1` compares the
entire captured RVFI prefix exactly against that observer-off run. A streaming
analysis checks flow retirement totals against the independent progress counters;
its bounded windows do not infer logical-hart identity from the physical-core RVFI
filename.

At the LSU boundary the sequence is unambiguous:

- TID3/generation9571 is a wrong-path stack load, allocated at cycle118623.
- A branch mispredict cancels it at118627; the scoreboard drops it at118628.
- At118632 the same TID is allocated to a byte load as generation9572.
- At118644 the old stack request returns a full return-address value under TID3.
  The scoreboard accepts it for generation9572, which retires at118645.
- The real byte-load result arrives at118647, after that instruction has retired.
  The adjacent old load similarly returns under the replacement branch's TID.

`load_unit` already retains cancelled state **after cache grant**, but the
ungranted request remained in `lsu_bypass` while store-address pressure blocked
it. That queue did not retain the cancellation mask. Once the scoreboard reused
the TID, the late cache grant saw a live replacement ID and allocated an unflushed
response slot. Thus the earlier claim that late-TID stimulus was unreachable is
**superseded**: the survey omitted the pre-grant owner, not the response buffer.

**Repair and contract.** Under `OoOEn`, every valid queued load remembers exact
scoreboard cancellation in its existing `is_speculative_load_miss` bit. That bit
survives release of the scoreboard cancel mask and clears with normal queue
replacement/reset/flush. `load_unit` discards such a head without a cache request,
translation request, forwarded completion or synthetic writeback. The SEND_TAG /
abort handshake for an already accepted older request and unrelated responses are
preserved. OoO no longer derives this bit from an unrelated branch resolution;
the exact cancellation mask is authoritative. The in-order path remains unchanged.
No generation bits are added to FU interfaces, and no permission check is bypassed.

**Evidence.** `tb_g6lc_review_load_cancel` drives the live bypass/load-unit seam.
`ooo-loadcancel-before-v2` reproduces `LOAD_CANCEL_STALE_REQUEST` while its
post-grant-cancel and full-flush controls pass. `ooo-loadcancel-after-v2` has
42 matched positive/negative records over two/four load slots plus OoO-off
controls: head/tail retention, same-cycle cancel/grant, WAIT_GNT, old responses
with reused TIDs, older SEND_TAG preservation, full flush and unrelated branch
resolution. Removing just queued cancellation in a copied source recreates the
failure (`ooo-loadcancel-mutation-v1`). The expanded matrix
`ooo-loadcancel-translation-v1` has54 matched records including translation abort,
speculative-load waiting, forwarded cancellation and a concurrent older response.
These are directed checks, not validation of every FU's cancellation lifecycle.
`ooo-loadcancel-formal-v1` additionally passes an eight-step live-bypass check
against an independent shift-queue reference (two entries, four TIDs) and reaches
cancel -> mask release -> cancelled drain. The same source mutation produces a
counterexample in `ooo-loadcancel-formal-mutation-v1`. This is bounded queue safety,
not an unbounded proof or full LSU/cache liveness result.

On the repaired two-hart model, the identical firmware remains active at200k:
hart1 retires72,561 instead of42,592, with no recorded access fault or WFI park.
At2M it has458,166 retirements and is in the HSM wait path; hart0 continues with
1,000,521 retirements. This clears the original failure but is not boot completion.
The subsequent full run on this first candidate (`ooo-osbi-loadcancel-full-v1`,
model6fee531c...) reached the OpenSBI1.5 banner, HART Count2 and domain enumeration,
then stopped at simulation time10,707,552 on `load_unit.addr_offset0`. The runner
records `outcome:error`, `strictDualPassed:false`. Its trace tail is retained in
`ooo-osbi-loadcancel-assert-tail-v1`; it follows UART console operations, but neither
the faulting request's ownership nor whether the assertion is overrestrictive is
established by that tail. Do not suppress the assertion or call the banner a boot.

A self-critique then caught a missing correct-prediction release in the first
candidate: `ooo-loadcancel-correct-resolve-before-v1` fails its new scenario12.
The final queue rule suppresses only broad mispredict marking; it preserves the
existing correct-prediction release. `ooo-loadcancel-final-leaf-v1` passes56
records, and final eight-step safety/cover plus a retention-mutation counterexample
pass (`ooo-loadcancel-final-formal{,-mutation}-v1`). Both changed units pass live-port
synthesis with zero latches/SCCs (`ooo-loadcancel-final-synth-v1`); that is scoped
leaf synthesis, not whole-core timing. The full firmware measurement above predates
this last refinement and remains attributed to its own model. Final firmware,
protected-anchor and structural gate results remain separate; no guarded
configuration is promoted here.

Final integer model315f6a05... passes19 directed/regression Spike comparisons
(`ooo-loadcancel-final-s*`, `-memdep-v1`, `-ilp-v1`). Final FP fence/status remain
1469/1253 with matching Spike rows, and their negative fails986. Final guarded
startup/LR-SC/lock remain663/582/5883, with the negative failing475. The final
200k firmware replay (`ooo-osbi-loadcancel-final-bounded-v1`, model9df317d5...)
reproduces54,317/72,561 retirements with neither hart faulted/parked; it is still
correctly classified as a bounded timeout, not boot completion. Final source lints at10 warnings
against baseline11. The protected boot `smt2-loadcancel-protected-v1` passes at
12,765,628 cycles and333,635/8,932,406 retirements; the post-refinement in-order
rebuild has the identical executable SHA256
`f35d0e10afd10fcb1594da94cd0a02f0a14744d776a4852ffd8de61393be9536`.
A full-core synthesis of an earlier candidate finished clean with2 warnings but
predates the final refinement; a new final-source run is in flight. Strict
standalone slang remains unavailable, so no aggregate all-gates PASS is claimed.

**Instrument repair.** The firmware runner now records explicit
pass/fail/timeout/error/incomplete outcomes and cannot accept a cap banner as
completion. It accepts the builder's native `build-review.json` without copying
or renaming it, rejects conflicting hashes and incomplete qualification metadata,
and retains the full original/review source-hash map plus manifest digest.
Positive and refusal tests cover both experimental and default provenance.

**Corrections to earlier generalizations.** LR retirement does not expire an ISA
reservation; closing an implementation issue window is distinct from clearing that
reservation. Stage31 contains five stores, so its PASS does not prove capacity
coverage for an eight-entry queue. Unequal boot retirement counts are not a
fairness measurement. The final access-fault pin was downstream of a demonstrated
load-identity violation, not evidence selecting the frontend as the repair owner.
Synthesis smoke does not prove STA closure or a negligible critical-path cost.

**SoC review.** The added cone is a TID-indexed cancellation lookup at each of the
two existing bypass entries and head control; the existing tombstone field is
reused, with no new array, pipeline stage, clock, reset or ISA/CSR/DTS capability.
The 1.25GHz/12nm target remains inferred; STA and power/DFT sign-off remain open.
The six-channel audit affects wrong-path completion and retained cancellation
(channels1/3), preserves surviving responses (2/4), adds no value-based control
(5), and does not claim mixed-hart memory ordering (6). Tier-U notices in LSU RTL
are retained; tooling/tests remain MIT. The named licensing diagnostic is still
unregistered, so manual tier/header review is not called an automated PASS.

### FP lifetime and guarded SMT2+OoO foundation (2026-09-19)

The user has reopened the previously deferred SMT2+OoO work. The first measured
milestone is **coarse-handoff, two-hart integer OoO in an isolated build**, not
mixed residency, an enabled SKU, or Linux/OpenSBI qualification. Repository FP and
multi-hart OoO guards remain active; their messages now describe pending release
qualification rather than claiming that no simulation exists.

This continuation repaired the following independently reproduced contracts:

| Boundary | Defect and retained repair |
|---|---|
| FP commit -> recovery map | Dispatch excluded FP writes from rename's committed-map update. Include FP destinations, including f0; a full flush must restore the committed FP mapping. |
| FP physical storage | The integer PRF's hardwired physical-zero rule was reused for FP. `g6lc_prf.ZERO_REG_ZERO` defaults true; only the FP instance selects false. FP physical zero is writable and retains data beyond WB bypass. |
| Hart-local rename recovery | FP had no physical-owner reclaim path. Record FP physical owners and restore only the affected hart's committed map; preserve peer speculative allocations and busy bits. |
| Same-cycle ownership transfer | Integer recovery consulted yesterday's owner after reallocating a free register to the peer. Reclaim against the post-allocation owner, not the stale registered owner. |
| Dispatch -> rename namespaces | Rename supported multiple harts, but dispatch tied both dispatch and commit hart to zero. Wire SBE hart identity, instantiate all namespaces, and bound physical/checkpoint geometry. Shared pools are retained. |
| Completion -> operand availability | CSR/AMO execution WB was waking consumers with a placeholder. Keep raw completion for retirement tracking, but wake rename/IQ only when real data is available, including the existing commit-time PRF mirror. IQ wakeup count is parameterized; no additional PRF data-write port is added. |
| WFI retirement -> drained handoff | WFI could halt commit with younger allocated work still in the window. An accepted OoO WFI now uses the existing precise flush/next-PC path before parking. Controller honors this independently of RVA. |
| Full flush -> speculative-store lifetime | Legacy SMT keep/replay rules retained a younger store across a fence, although its scoreboard allocation was discarded. `LEGACY_SMT_KEEP` excludes OoO: full flush drops speculative stores, cancellation cannot be overridden by the keep bit, and the old retained forwarding cache is inactive. Committed stores survive. |

The last two defects are distinct and both were observed. Before WFI recovery,
hart0 WFI retired at cycle599, younger `(tid0,gen22)` completed at601 but could not
retire, and no handoff followed. With WFI recovery, the handoff occurs at time621
and the peer resumes. The completion store still did not reach its intended queue
entry: store tid2/gen12, offered at506, survived the fence's full flush at513. Its
replay tid1/gen15 retired at522 but the buffer committed **tid2**. Subsequent commits
were displaced; the completion-store retirement committed an older address instead.
With OoO store recovery, the completion store commits its own tid7 at `0x80001000`.
This is identity-tagged evidence, not inference from a truncated store log.

#### OoO memory ordering in the LSU store buffer (2026-09-20)

Two further contracts were violated under OoO issue, found by directed tests rather
than by a failing workload, and both are repaired in `core/store_buffer.sv` behind
`CVA6Cfg.OoOEn`. The in-order path is unchanged by construction and re-qualified.

| Contract | Violation | Repair |
|---|---|---|
| A store is observable only by a load that follows it in program order | The hazard and forward paths matched on address alone (`page_offset_matches_o`, `st_fwd_merge`). Under in-order issue a younger store could never precede an older load in the buffer; under OoO it can. | Speculative entries and the in-flight offer are qualified by circular trans_id distance from the oldest live instruction. Commit-queue entries are deliberately exempt: they are architecturally older and their tids may already be recycled. |
| Stores become visible in program order | The speculative queue is written at AGU time, so arrival order is issue order, and `commit_i` popped that FIFO head. | Arrivals are placed at their program-order position, restoring the head-is-oldest invariant the forward scan, `rvfi_mem_paddr_o` and the commit assertions already assumed. |

The age filter on the hazard term is required for **liveness**, not only for data:
commit is in order, so an older load that stalls on a younger store can never
progress — that store cannot commit until the load retires. Stage19 is that case
and it **hung** before the repair; it is not a wrong-value test.

New inputs `load_trans_id_i` and `commit_trans_id_i` reuse the existing
`commit_tran_id_i` anchor already routed to `load_unit`; no new commit-stage signal,
ISA surface, clock, reset or memory port is introduced. Two OoO-only assertions were
added: the committing store must be the queue head, and no younger speculative entry
may contribute to `st_fwd_be_o`.

Evidence, all on model `a1fe48a78e937be4…` (`g6lc64_ooo_int`, warnings 9 = baseline):

- Directed stages17/18/19 in `ooo_mem_min.S`, added to `testlist_ooo_l3.yaml`:
  before **FAIL 929 / FAIL 949 / TIMEOUT at the 10,000-cycle cap**; after
  **PASS 915 / 940 / 952**, each matching Spike's ordered retirements
  (`ooo-memorder-{before,after}-s{17,18,19}-v1`).
- Wrong-expectation negatives fail as required: **928 / 943 / 938**
  (`ooo-memorder-afterneg-s*-v1`).
- The in-order control passes all three on the identical frozen ELFs
  (`ooo-memorder-inorder-s*-v1`). Its retirement comparison is not used: the
  dual-hart model reports 51 rows against Spike's 20, an instrument scope artifact,
  so the self-checking verdict is the discriminator there.
- Identity-tagged before-traces name the mechanisms rather than inferring them:
  store_commit id=8 (0x333) at cycle872 ahead of id=7 (0x222) at873; and load tid7
  writing back 0x222 forwarded from store tid8.
- Leaf fixture `ooo-storeorder-after-v1`: **32 matched records** (24 previous plus
  new scenarios4/5 positive and negative on both OoO configurations).
- Non-regression on the same model: S4/S5/S15/memdep/ILP unchanged at
  **878/974/883/1057/1109**, all Spike-matched (`ooo-memorder-regr-*-v1`).
- FP suite unchanged: **1471 / 1251** pass with **199 / 200** matched rows, negative
  fails at **988** with 25 (`ooo-memorder-fp-*-v1`).
- Guarded dual-hart unchanged: startup **PASS664** (47/73), LR/SC **586 / 582**,
  negative **FAIL475** (`ooo-smt2-startup-positive-v5`, `ooo-smt2-lrsc-*-v2`).

Two further directed cases were added as self-critique of the repair itself, since
an age filter can fail by excluding too much rather than too little:

- Stage20, a younger load against an older same-address store whose **data** is
  late: the load must wait for it, not read around it. **PASS911**, Spike-matched;
  negative fails933; in-order passes. This is the case a wrongly over-filtering
  age test would break, and stages17-19 could not detect it.
- Stage21, five younger stores queued ahead of a data-delayed oldest store.
  **PASS930**, Spike-matched. The trace confirms the precondition really occurred
  rather than the test passing vacuously: offers at cycles789-805 for ids
  8,10,12,14,16, the oldest (id6) only at **850**, and commits nevertheless in
  program order 6,8,10,12,14,16 at851-856.

#### Repaired: speculative store queue capacity inversion (2026-09-20)

Stage21 did not reach capacity (`DeepSpecEn=1`, `MaxOutstandingStores=8`, so
`DEPTH_SPEC=8`). Stage22 raises it to ten outstanding stores with the oldest
data-delayed, and **deadlocks**:

```
store_offer cycle=789..809  ids 18,20,22,24,26,28,30   ready=1   eight younger stores
store_offer cycle=811       id=0                       ready=0   queue full, refused
the oldest store (0x601) never posts; no store_commit ever occurs
```

The speculative queue drains **only** on commit and commit is in program order, so
once younger stores occupy every slot the oldest store can never post, nothing can
retire, and nothing can drain. It hangs at the 20,000-cycle cap while the harness
again prints `*** SUCCESS ***`; `outcome`/`timedOut` are the real verdict.

**This is pre-existing, not a regression of the ordering repair.** The identical
frozen ELF also times out on the previous model `4fc4cc5…`
(`ooo-memorder-before-s22-v1`), and the in-order control passes at1792 cycles.
Occupancy is unchanged by program-order insertion; only placement changed.

It is a liveness defect, so it cannot be fixed by enlarging the queue — any depth
can be exceeded. Gating late, in `store_buffer.ready_o` or in `store_unit`, also
fails: a refused younger store then occupies the one-deep store pipe and blocks
the older store from issuing at all. Admission therefore belongs at **issue**.

**Repair: stores issue in program order relative to each other** (`core/ooo/g6lc_iq.sv`).
A store is not selected while another store older than it is still waiting in the
IQ. This is an admission rule over *unissued* entries, which is why it does not
reproduce the deadlock the file already warns about for a live-store gate: the
oldest unissued store never has an older unissued store, so it always proceeds.
Loads are untouched and still issue out of order.

Chosen over IQ capacity reservation (free-slot count fed back into issue) because
it needs no new cross-module signal on the issue path, and stores cannot retire
out of order anyway, so the expected ILP loss is small. That expectation is borne
out: every regression cycle count is unchanged and stages17-21 move by at most one
cycle.

Evidence on model `d6a04888816a58ab…` (warnings9 = baseline):
stage22 **TIMEOUT -> PASS933**, Spike-matched; stages17-21 **915/940/952/911/931**,
all Spike-matched; S4/S5/S15/memdep/ILP unchanged at **878/974/883/1057/1109**;
leaf fixture **32 matched records**; recovery prove+cover both pass; FP
**1471/1251** with negative **988**; guarded dual-hart **664** (47/73), LR/SC
**586/582**, negative **475**. `g6lc_ooo_dispatch` — and therefore the IQ — is
instantiated only inside `if (CVA6Cfg.OoOEn)` in `issue_stage.sv`, so the in-order
path cannot be affected by this rule. That is not left as an argument: rebuilding
`g6lc64_smt2` after the change reproduces executable
`834214d04daf69a9…`, byte-identical to the model that passed the protected
OpenSBI boot above, so the12,765,628-cycle result carries over rather than needing
another firmware run.

With stores admitted in program order, the program-order insertion in
`store_buffer` is now **defence in depth** rather than the sole guarantee: arrivals
already come in order on the LSU path. It is retained because it is what the
`ooo_commit_is_oldest` assertion checks and it costs no measured cycles, but it is
a candidate for removal if STA shows the insertion crossbar on the store write
path is expensive. That is a deliberate, flagged redundancy, not an accident.

#### Instrument defect, not a core defect: RVFI operand capture under OoO

Probing AMO ordering produced an apparent fourth defect and then retracted it. At
`amoadd.d`, the RTL trace logged the memory effect as the **pre-operation** value
(0x700 where Spike had 0x705) while returning the correct old value in `rd` — and
the program's own read-back still saw the added value. A contradiction like that
indicts the observation before the core.

The discriminator was a second `amoadd.d rd, x0, (addr)`: an AMO reads memory at
commit and cannot be satisfied by a forward, so its returned value is authoritative.
It returned **0x807** after a first AMO on 0x800+7, as did a following load
(`ooo-amoorder-after-s24-v1`, PASS950). **Memory is correct and AMO ordering is
sound**; stages23 and24 pass on their own checks.

The defect is in `core/cva6_rvfi.sv`. It captures
`rs1_rdata`/`rs2_rdata` into `mem_n[issue_pointer[i]]` gated on
`decoded_instr_valid[i] && decoded_instr_ack[i]`, i.e. at **dispatch**, while
`rvfi_rs1_o`/`rvfi_rs2_o` are `fu_data_n[i].operand_a/b` from
`issue_read_operands`, i.e. the operands at **issue**, indexed by issue port.
In order those are the same instruction in the same cycle. Under OoO the IQ issues
a different, older ready entry than the one being dispatched, so the two channels
are joined by port index and cycle proximity when identity no longer supports that
join. `rs2_rdata` therefore arrives as another instruction's operand (0 here), and
`compute_amo_wdata` — whose own arithmetic is correct — adds 0.

Consequences, stated precisely:
- RVFI `rs1_rdata`/`rs2_rdata` were unreliable under OoO generally, and AMO
  `mem_wdata` is derived from `rs2_rdata`, so **AMO memory effects were not actually
  checked by trace comparison** until the repair below. That was a blind spot in the
  method used throughout this record; it is recorded rather than worked around.
- It does **not** retract earlier results. Those comparisons matched on PC,
  encoding, privilege, register writes and store effects, which come from other
  fields, and none of the compared programs contains an AMO.
- Stages23/24 were initially registered as program-self-checking only; masking the
  field in the comparator would have been weakening a check to make a run green.
  After the repair they are trace-compared as well.

**Repaired.** `issue_read_operands` now exports `rvfi_operand_valid_o` /
`rvfi_operand_tid_o` — the issuing entry's `trans_id` alongside the operands it is
actually reading — carried through `issue_stage`, `cva6.sv` and the
`rvfi_probes_instr_t` struct. Under `OoOEn`, `cva6_rvfi` re-captures
`rs1_rdata`/`rs2_rdata` against that identity after the dispatch-time write. The
in-order capture path is untouched and still used when `OoOEn` is 0.

With the instrument repaired, the AMO memory effect is checked for the first time:
stages23 and24 now report `retirementsMatch: true, qualified: true` against Spike
(`ooo-rvfi-amo-s23-v1` PASS928 /19 rows, `ooo-rvfi-amo-s24-v1` PASS950 /20 rows) —
the same programs whose AMO row previously diverged. Model `6180bbc8e219402c…`,
warnings9 = baseline. All thirteen directed and regression programs re-checked on
that model pass with ordered Spike matching and unchanged cycle counts
(`ooo-rvfi-s17..22`, `ooo-rvfi-regr-*`): 915/940/952/911/931/933 and
878/974/883/1057/1109.

A second, independent instrument fault surfaced immediately afterwards, on the
same principle. Stage25 (LR/SC against a still-speculative older store) **passes
its own checks at956 cycles** — LR observes the older store, the SC succeeds and
memory ends with the SC value — but the trace showed a spurious memory write of 0
for the `lr.d` where Spike shows none. `lsu_wmask` was
`instr.lsu_ctrl_fu == STORE ? be : 0`, and every AMO takes the STORE path,
including load-reserved, which only reads. The probe struct now also carries
`lsu_ctrl_op` so the mask can exclude `AMO_LRW`/`AMO_LRD`. After that,
**all fourteen directed and regression programs match Spike**, stage25 included
(`ooo-rvfi2-*`, model `3d1a304213e29328…`, warnings9 = baseline), with every cycle
count unchanged. A failed SC writes no memory either; that case has no directed
test yet and is left named rather than assumed correct.

The probe struct gained fields for every configuration, so the in-order executable
hash necessarily changes and the byte-identity argument used earlier does not apply
here. The protected boot was therefore re-run rather than assumed:
`smt2-rvfi-dualboot-v2` on model `57e2c4809a7d4dc2…` reports
`strictDualPassed=true` at exactly **12,765,628 cycles** and **333,635/8,932,406**
retirements — the anchor values. An earlier attempt aborted in 0.3s with
`another harness is active`, which is the runner's concurrency guard refusing to
measure while other jobs ran, not a boot failure; it was re-run alone.

The LR write-mask correction changes the struct again, so the boot was re-run once
more on the final RTL: `smt2-rvfi2-dualboot-v1`, model
`45dd63fb6265869c…`, `strictDualPassed=true`, **12,765,628 cycles**,
**333,635/8,932,406**. Full-core lint and synthesis were re-run after each RTL
increment, most recently after the CSR commit-head admission rule — i.e. against
the final RTL of this work: **lint PASS at 10 warnings against baseline11** and
**synthesis PASS, clean with 2 warnings** (~51min). Earlier synthesis runs in this
session predate later edits and must not be cited for the final state. Strict standalone-slang is
still unavailable on the builder, so the aggregate gate continues to report
incomplete and no skip waiver is taken. Earlier synthesis runs in this session
predate later edits and must not be cited for this state.

#### Repaired: a store following a load-reserved never retired (2026-09-20)

Probing the failed-SC case found a further, unrelated hang. Stage26 (LR, an
intervening same-hart store, then SC) **times out** under OoO at the 20,000-cycle
cap while the in-order control passes at1751.

The stuck instruction is identified, not inferred. Retirement proceeds normally
through the `lr.d` itself:

```
retire cycle=803 id=0 pc=0x800001ec   lr.d  t1,(sp)      <- the LR retires
retire cycle=804 id=1 pc=0x800001f0   lui   t2,0x1
retire cycle=804 id=2 pc=0x800001f2   addiw t2,t2,-1535
(nothing further; next instruction is sd t2,0(sp) at 0x800001f6)
```

Stage27 is the discriminator: the same shape with the store aimed at an
**unrelated address** (`16(sp)`) also times out, while in-order passes at1736.
So this is **not** reservation logic and not same-address ordering — it is any
plain store that follows a load-reserved.

**Pre-existing, and not a consequence of this session's work.** The identical
frozen ELF times out on all four model generations: the pre-change baseline
`work-ver-ooo-foundation-int-v2`, the memory-order model, the store-admission
model and the final RVFI model (`ooo-scfail-attrib-{pre,memorder,storeadmit}-v1`).

It also explains why the existing dual-hart LR/SC tests pass: those sequences put
the `sc.d` directly after the `lr.d` with no intervening plain store, which is the
case that works.

A finer trace refines the symptom and **weakens the first hypothesis**:

```
alloc  cycle=798 id=3 pc=0x800001f6 fu=2 op=38    the sd is allocated
retire cycle=803 id=0 pc=0x800001ec               the LR retires
alloc  ... ids 4..26 continue well past it ...
stalled cycle=1828 issue_ptr=27 commit_ptr=3 full=0 decoded=11 ack=00
```

So the store is allocated, becomes the commit head (`commit_ptr=3`), and simply
never writes back; ~24 instructions allocate behind it and dispatch then stalls
with the ROB not full. No `store_offer` appears after the LR, so the store never
reaches the store buffer.

Attribution then took two corrections, each forced by an observation rather than by
argument.

First hypothesis: the depth-1 amo_buffer is never released, holding
`st_ready = store_buffer_ready & amo_buffer_ready` low. I then *weakened* that by
reasoning that the FIFO pops on `amo_resp_i.ack` and the LR retired, so the ack
must have occurred. A temporary store-unit probe showed that reasoning was wrong:

```
cycle=800..806  st_ready=0  sb_rdy=1  amo_rdy=0    amo buffer occupied; store buffer fine
```

`retire => ack => pop` was an inference, not an observation, and it did not hold.

Extending the probe to the AMO handshake then corrected the picture again:

```
cycle=789..799  amo_req=1 amo_ack=0 amo_commit=1 nostp=1
cycle=800       amo_req=1 amo_ack=1                 the ack does arrive
(probe silent afterwards: st_ready=1, FSM idle, valid_i=0)
```

So the AMO buffer **does** drain, `st_ready` recovers, and the store unit then sits
idle and ready while `valid_i` never rises. **The store unit is measured and
cleared: the store is withheld upstream and never presented to the LSU at all.**
That matches the scoreboard trace, where the store is allocated, becomes the commit
head and never writes back.

Stage28 (the same shape with the fence removed) hangs identically, so neither the
fence flush nor the re-push into the amo_buffer is required. Stages26, 27 and 28
all time out on **all four model generations**, including the pre-change baseline
`work-ver-ooo-foundation-int-v2`, so none of this session's changes — in particular
not the program-order store-admission rule — is implicated
(`ooo-scfail-attrib-*`, `ooo-s28-attrib-*`).

The upstream owner was then found by reading the issue path, and confirmed by the
`issue_stall` diagnostic that already existed in `issue_read_operands`:

```
issue_stall idle=1024 valid=11 ack=00 busy=10 raw=00 rs1=00 rs2=00
            flu_ready=1 lsu_ready=1 casq=0  lrsc=1
```

Every other term is ready; `lrsc=1` is the only asserted blocker, and it is stuck.
That is `lr_sc_pair_q`, whose own comment states the intent: *"LR→SC exclusive pair
window. Set on LR issue ack; clear on SC issue or pipeline flush. While live, block
non-SC STORE issue so intervening stores cannot clear AXI exclusive reservation."*
It exists so an intervening store cannot break the AXI reservation that OpenSBI's
cmpxchg depends on.

**Root cause.** The window is closed only by an SC issue or a flush. Under in-order
issue one of those always arrives while the LR is still live, so the window is
inherently bounded — the in-order model passes all three stages. Out of order it is
not bounded: an LR that is never followed by an SC, which is perfectly legal RISC-V,
leaves the window open and every later non-SC store is refused an issue
acknowledge forever.

**Repair** (`core/ooo/../issue_read_operands.sv`, gated on `OoOEn`): remember the
`trans_id` of the LR that opened the window and close it when that LR is no longer
live, using the existing `fwd_i.still_issued` (`issued & ~cancelled`). That is
exactly the point at which no younger SC can still complete the reservation, so it
bounds the window without weakening the guard while the pair is genuinely in
flight. No new port and no change to the in-order path.

Result: stages26/27/28 complete at **896/880/873** cycles instead of timing out,
and stages17-25 plus S4/S5/S15/memdep/ILP are unchanged and still Spike-matched.
Stage26 remains self-checking only: with a same-hart store between LR and SC the
sequence is unconstrained, so the SC may legally succeed or fail — CVA6 fails it,
Spike succeeds — and the test asserts only that the reported result and the memory
content agree.

That stage also caught the third instance of the RVFI write-mask class, the one
previously named as untested: a **failed** SC was still reporting a memory write
(`rd=1` with a write of 0xA02) while the program's read-back proved memory was
untouched. `mem_wmask` is now suppressed at commit when an SC reports failure, and
the RTL row becomes an empty memory-effect list, matching the architecture.

Because this change touches LR/SC handling directly, the suites that exercise it
were re-run on the repaired model rather than assumed unaffected: the guarded
dual-hart startup is unchanged at **664** cycles (47/73), LR/SC RS1/RS2/ALU at
**586/586/582** with the wrong-result control still failing at **475**, and the FP
suite at **1471/1251** with **199/200** matched Spike rows and its negative failing
at **988** with 25. The protected in-order OpenSBI boot reproduces
**12,765,628** cycles and **333,635/8,932,406**. 44 host tests pass.

The temporary store-unit probe has been removed; `store_unit.sv` carries only the
intended port additions.

#### Repaired: an uncommitted CSR wedged the whole fixed-latency unit (2026-09-20)

With the memory-order and LR/SC repairs in place, the existing `SMT_LOCK` mode of
`smt_dual_active.S` — both harts contending for one `amoswap`-based lock and doing
identical critical-section work — was run on the guarded dual-hart OoO model for
the first time. It **times out at 200,000 cycles with only 35/39 retirements**,
i.e. both harts stop; this is a stall, not spin starvation, since a spinning hart
would retire thousands of iterations. The in-order SMT2 control passes the same
frozen ELF in **7,392 cycles with 1,600/2,037 retirements**, showing real
interleaving (`ooo-smt2-lock-{both,pause}-v1`, `ooo-smt2-lock-inorder-v1`).

The trace narrows it without yet naming an owner:

```
issue_stall valid=11 ack=00 busy=11 raw=00 flu_ready=0 lsu_ready=1 lrsc=0 live=11111111
last retire: hart0 pc=0x80000066, hart1 pc=0x80000060
```

`flu_ready=0` permanently with **every scoreboard entry live**, and both harts are
still in the prologue — they never reach the lock loop at all, so this is not
contention over the lock itself. `lrsc=0` also shows the LR/SC window repair above
is not involved.

Two single-hart candidates were tested and did **not** reproduce it:
- stage29, `amoswap.w.aq` followed by a store — the lock's critical-section shape —
  passes at **893** cycles, Spike-matched. The store-after-AMO path is clear.
- stage30, a younger CSR issued ahead of an older FLU consumer waiting on a slow
  divide (ALU/branch/CSR share the FLU, and a CSR resolves at commit) passes at
  **896** cycles, Spike-matched.

Neither is a disproof — a directed program may simply not create the exact
interleaving — but both ruled out the shape they model.

**Root cause, read from the code rather than inferred.** `csr_buffer` is a
**depth-1, hart-agnostic** structure whose comment states the intent ("a second CSR
must not be accepted before the first retires"), and it holds `csr_ready_o` low
from issue until the CSR **commits**:

```systemverilog
if ((csr_reg_q.valid || csr_valid_i) && ~csr_commit_i) csr_ready_o = 1'b0;
```

`ex_stage` then computes `flu_ready_o = csr_ready & mult_ready`, and
`issue_read_operands` turns `!flu_ready_i` into *every FLU unit is busy* — ALU and
branch included, for **every hart**. So one uncommitted CSR stops all
fixed-latency issue. In order this is harmless: a CSR is effectively the oldest
instruction when it issues, so it commits promptly. Out of order it need not be —
younger work fills the scoreboard while the CSR waits behind older instructions
that themselves need the FLU, and nothing can retire. That is exactly the observed
state: `flu_ready=0`, `live=11111111`, both harts stalled in the barrier.

**Repair** (`core/ooo/g6lc_iq.sv`, OoO-only by construction): a CSR is selected
only when it is the oldest live instruction, `sbe.trans_id == commit_ptr_i`. That
restores the depth-1 buffer's own assumption instead of weakening it, and reuses
the commit anchor the IQ already has — no new port, no change to the in-order path.
It is the same shape as the store-admission and LR/SC-window repairs above: a
resource held until commit must not be handed to work that can be overtaken.

**The two-hart lock test now passes on OoO at 5,883 cycles with 1,600/1,952
retirements** — faster than the in-order control's 7,392 — so two-hart mutual
exclusion works. The cost is a uniform **+3 cycles** on the directed and regression
set (881/977/886/1060/1112 against 878/974/883/1057/1109), which is the expected
price of serialising a rare, already-serialising instruction class. All nineteen
directed and regression programs still match Spike; the guarded dual-hart startup
(663, 47/73), LR/SC (586/582) and its negative control (475) are unchanged, and the
FP suite holds at 1469/1253 with 199/200 rows and its negative at 986.

This bounds the dual-hart OoO milestone concretely and should be read alongside the
startup and LR/SC passes above: the guarded model completes reset rendezvous and
LR/SC consumer sequences, but **cannot currently run a mutual-exclusion workload
between the two harts**. Since OpenSBI and Linux rely on spin locks, that is a
prerequisite for any firmware-level dual-hart OoO claim, and none is made.

#### Self-critique: the two IQ admission rules must compose

Program-order store admission and CSR commit-head admission are independent gates
on the same selection loop, and each can hold an entry back indefinitely in
principle. Neither existing test exercises both at once, so a composition failure
would have been invisible. Stage31 interleaves CSRs with five stores whose
**oldest** has late data: the stores may not overtake it, and the CSRs may not
issue until they are oldest — which cannot happen until that stalled store drains.
If the rules composed badly this deadlocks rather than merely slowing.

It passes at **906** cycles, Spike-matched, with the wrong-expectation control
failing at942 (`ooo-bothadmit-s31-*`). Registered as `ooo_admission_interaction`.

**Timing impact of the IQ admission terms** (§0.2 note, pending real STA). Per
entry the selection loop now evaluates: the pre-existing `older_st` scan
(`NR_SB_ENTRIES` circular-distance comparisons against `commit_ptr_i`), the
`older_unissued_st` scan added for store admission (`DEPTH` comparisons of the same
form), and the CSR term, which is a **single `TRANS_ID_BITS`-wide equality** against
`commit_ptr_i`. The CSR term is therefore negligible next to the two scans; the
store scan is the one that widens the cone, and it is `DEPTH` comparators feeding
the same OR-reduction that `older_st` already drove. All three are ANDed into the
existing grant condition rather than lengthening it serially. Synthesis remains
clean with the baseline two warnings and no new latches, but that is elaboration
evidence, not timing closure — the store-path comparators and these terms still
need an STA pass, which is recorded as an open item.

#### Firmware on dual-hart OoO: now measurable, and it does NOT boot

The harness now has an explicit experimental-model mode
(`SOURCE_REVIEW_EXPERIMENTAL=1`) that admits an isolated qualification build while
**carrying its provenance into the result** rather than hiding it. The identity
binding is unchanged and mandatory in every mode — the measured executable must be
the hash the manifest attests; only the field *name* (`modelSha256` beside
`executableSha256`) is now accepted. Two new refusals keep the modes honest: a
substituted model without the flag is rejected, and the flag without a substituted
model is rejected. The default refusal was verified
(`ooo-opensbi-guardctl-v3`: *"substituted qualification model requires
SOURCE_REVIEW_EXPERIMENTAL=1"*), so the guard still holds for the protected anchor.

First measurement (`ooo-opensbi-experimental-v1`), correctly self-labelled
`experimentalModel: true`, `protectedAnchor: false`, `modelQualificationOnly: true`,
`modelHarts: 2`, substituting `config_pkg.sv`, `g6lc64_smt2_config_pkg.sv`,
`g6lc_ooo_dispatch.sv`, `g6lc_rename.sv`:

| | in-order anchor | dual-hart OoO |
|---|---|---|
| outcome | `strictDualPassed: true` | **`strictDualPassed: false`** |
| cycles | 12,765,628 | **hit the 16,000,000 cap** |
| hart0 / hart1 retirements | 333,635 / 8,932,406 | **9,805,054 / 42,592** |

**No firmware claim is made: this is a failure, not a boot.** The banner reads
`*** SUCCESS *** (tohost = 0) after 16000000 cycles`, but 16,000,000 is exactly the
cycle cap — the same misleading-banner pattern seen throughout this work, and the
reason the result record rather than the banner is authoritative.

Two concrete symptoms to pursue: the work distribution is *inverted* against the
anchor (there hart1 does the bulk; here hart0 does 9.8M while hart1 manages 42.6k),
and the hang pin reports `mcause1=0x1` with `wfi1=1` — hart1 took an **instruction
access fault** and parked. That is the next thread, and it is a firmware-scale
symptom, not one of the directed contracts repaired above.

#### How this became measurable (previously: blocked by a verification guard)

With two-hart mutual exclusion working, OpenSBI was attempted on the guarded OoO
dual-hart model. `run_opensbi_source_review.py` refused immediately with
`source-profile model identity mismatch`. That is the harness working as intended:
it binds the measured model to a build manifest by `executableSha256` and
`target`, so a boot can never be attributed to RTL other than the one attested.

The isolated OoO build does carry truthful provenance — `target: g6lc64_smt2`,
`harts: 2`, `qualificationOnly`, and a per-file original-vs-review SHA map naming
every guard substitution — but records the hash as `modelSha256`. Renaming that
field would pass the check while defeating its purpose, namely preventing an
experimental substituted build from being attributed as the protected
source-profile anchor. **It was deliberately not done, and no firmware result on
dual-hart OoO is claimed.**

The correct unblock is to give the runner an explicit experimental-model mode that
accepts the isolated build's own record and propagates `qualificationOnly` plus the
substitution map into the result, marking it as *not* the protected anchor. That
strengthens the identity binding rather than eroding it, and is a scoping decision
to take before implementing.

Limits: this qualifies same-word ordering and forwarding for the directed cases
above on a single hart. Cross-hart memory ownership, misaligned/partial-word
interactions beyond the `sb`/`ld` case, AMO/LR-SC ordering against speculative
stores, and the timing cost of the added age comparators on the store path are all
still open. The insertion re-order adds a small crossbar on the store write path and
needs an STA look before promotion; it is not on the load critical path.

All hardware/ISS runs use the remote proxy, VT1/fetch_B, pinned repaired runtime,
fresh run directories and retained source/ELF/model hashes.

  after passes at **1251**, matching **200 ordered retirements** against Spike.

The private NH2 model changes the copied SMT2 package to OoO1, RVF/RVD0 and

No ISA/CSR/DTS additions, new clock/reset, new pipeline stage or SRAM interface.

and ROB already carry SBE hart identity; lack of a separately named hart port alone

or a scheduling-performance claim. Licensing tiers/notices are preserved; no new

### Current two-issue recovery repair (2026-09-19)

This section supersedes the causal claims in the historical investigation below,
not its captured observations. The coding philosophy, runtime-learning guide,
complete SMT2 reasoning trio and active plan were re-read before this repair.
`fetch_A` / `smt_legacy` are historical evidence, not implementation templates or
permitted active build inputs. The active source remains `fetch_B` plus `core/smt`.

**Contract:** an accepted, non-cancelled instruction must remain owned by a queue
until execution accepts it; redirect suppression cannot acknowledge and discard
older work. Allocation, FU issue, completion and architectural retirement are
separate events. In-order issue implicitly couples allocation to execution;
OoO breaks that coupling, so its recovery handshake must state it explicitly.

**Measured first violation.** Existing `smt_flow_trace` records allocation generation.
The stage-15 head was `(hart=0, tid=4, generation=11)`, the load-result check at
`0x800001ec`, **not** the back-edge at `0x800001f4`. The younger back-edge (tid 7)
resolved a misprediction while the older load completed. At time 822, a bounded
additional observer captured tid 4 acknowledged at the IQ/IRO seam with
`cancelled=0`, `flush_unissued=1`, `full_flush=0`. IRO clears every outgoing FU-valid
on that redirect, but the IQ consumed its raw acknowledgement. The branch never
executed. Scoreboard `issued=1` means allocated, not FU-issued.

**Repair:** `g6lc_ooo_dispatch` suppresses FU offers on either flush and qualifies
IQ removal by the actual, externally visible `issue_valid_o`. New admission is
also blocked during full flush. Existing same-cycle cancellation still drops only
its selected identities; older entries and same-cycle writeback wakeups survive.
No changes to IQ selection, the store-age comparison, issue width, LSU forwarding,
branch resolution, or firmware. No blanket cancellation or synthetic completion.

Evidence from proxy runs, Verilator 5.008 VT1 and the pinned repaired runtime:

| Frozen test | Before | Repaired two-issue core |
|---|---|---|
| stage 15, four iterations | timeout | PASS 883 cycles |
| stage 5, sixteen iterations | timeout | PASS 974 cycles |
| `ooo_mem_dep` | timeout | PASS 1,057 cycles |
| stage 4 | PASS 878 | PASS 878 |
| `ooo_ilp_chain` | PASS 1,109 | PASS 1,109 |

`ooo-replay-{before,after}-{s5,s15,memdep}-v1` reuses the **identical ELF per pair**
with a 5,000-cycle cap. Earlier long-cap timeouts remain recorded. Model hashes:
before `e06f6d6fcb7476b9e659cdcaae19e2f0ba593b5cfc9ba8444c4b5b68f88e0493`,
after `862697d7ed510b841d0508d62cff5e878e0473c19d19a139f280ee008f3d1e22`.
The repaired stage-15 observer-on/off pair both completes at 883 cycles; its trace
shows the same tid/generation issue at time 823, WB at scoreboard cycle 811 and
retirement at cycle 812. All sixteen stages pass (`ooo-recovery-sweep-s*-v1`).
Artifacts are under `C:/Users/etcim/AppData/Local/Temp/cva6-artifacts/` and the named
immutable `/opt/testharness/runs/` directories; build manifests remain in `remote-runs/`.

The dispatch fixture gains recovery, concurrent wakeup, full-flush and selective-

The additional `spec_mispredict_chain.S` sweep found a **test ABI bug**: main called
its leaf without saving the incoming return address. The original frozen ELF times
out on both OoO and the in-order control. Saving/restoring ra in an aligned frame
repairs the oracle: unchanged nested-branch/call bodies pass on OoO1258 cycles and
in-order1291; a deliberately wrong final count fails on both (1260/1291). These are
`ooo-spec-oracle-fixed-{,inorder-}negative{0,1}-v1`. No DUT or firmware workaround
was added to accommodate that test.

**Corrections to prior inference:** empty IQ/LSQ does not mean an empty ROB;
absence of branch WB does not establish branch execution; four iterations did not
prove a circular-age bug; changing issue width and SuperscalarEn perturbed several
paths and did not exonerate rename/IQ/ROB. The port-0-only claim was false: EX muxes
branch data from the asserted branch port. No structural rewrite follows from the
mere presence of a vector-to-scalar mux without a reachable conflicting grant.

**SoC/visibility review:** existing OoOEn envelope, hart/FP guards unchanged. No new
state, clock/reset, SRAM, scan path, ISA/CSR/DTS field or PMU event. The added logic
is flush fanout plus per-port AND qualification, with no registered latency and no
acknowledgement-to-selection feedback. The recovery cycle was already unable to
execute; holding its queue entries does not remove legitimate execution bandwidth.
Channels 2/4 (lost required work / killed live state) are repaired; channels 1/3
(wrong-path effects / stale state) retain existing cancellation; no data-value
control is added; cross-hart ordering remains outside the guarded NH1 envelope.
Physical STA, power, broad architectural/reference comparison and FP/SMT OoO
qualification remain open. This is a contract repair, not production promotion.
Licensing review preserved tier-R/T headers and upstream attribution and reused the
recorded tier-R authorization. `diag run licensing` remains unregistered; manual
review is recorded rather than an invented automated licensing PASS.

### Completion assessment after SMT2/HSM (2026-09-19)

The passing firmware is a prerequisite and regression anchor, not evidence that
OoO execution is correct. The active SMT2 target uses OoOEn=0, one physical core,
two logical harts and a drained handoff. A fresh build after the OoO-only repairs
is byte-identical to the passing modelc421aedc... (`smt2-ooo-regate-build-20260919`).
The g6lc64_ooo target still requests RVF/RVD; the existing FP legality guard means
its name is not evidence of a usable qualified configuration. No guard is removed.

#### Shared-core arbitration development path

SMT siblings share physical capacity; balancing runnable task counts or equalizing
raw retirements is not the objective. Preserve useful per-core work subject to
forward-service and tail/QoS bounds. Distinguish kernel placement among physical
cores from the core's fetch/admission/issue arbitration among architectural harts.

Current seams constrain implementation:

- Fetch_B selects one hart, and cva6 blocks admission during a drained handoff.
  A biased fetch policy can first change safe-boundary preference, not fill another
  hart's unused lane while its predecessor is still draining.
- Dispatch admits an entire group across rename/ROB/IQ/LSQ. Reservation-station
  partitioning therefore needs coherent group credits and per-hart ownership first.
  Begin with static minimum reservations; lend only free capacity from an elastic
  pool, and refund once on issue/cancellation. Never reclaim live allocations.
- IQ is compacting, oldest-ready, and stops when its selected candidate is not
  acknowledged. FU-aware fallback and mixed-hart partial groups need separate
  handshake/age/recovery proofs, not just a different priority comparator.
- QoS should budget new speculative/demand/prefetch admission, never completions,
  older stores, invalidations or required interrupt progress. Optional hard rate
  limits must distinguish intentional idling from unused capacity available to peers.

The current core PMU is a single counter/selector block without a hart dimension;
sim-only scheduling traces are not a software fairness API. Before advertising
minimal HPM/SBI hints, qualify ownership, privilege/inhibit/overflow and context
switch semantics. Prefer small programmable event pools (eligible-unselected time,
accepted work, coarse resource pressure) and software-derived window ratios over
per-station telemetry or new always-on banks. Control hints remain privileged,
coarse and optional with static fallback; no ABI or legality guard is changed here.

The active plan's B1 and Phases1b/4/6/8/9 make these explicit gates. Its measurement
path separates selected cycles from accepted work, checks common overlap versus
single-worker tails, and compares identical code with solo controls on each hart.
No SMT2/HSM or fixed-work result enables mixed-hart OoO by itself.

#### Bounded increment: result ownership

`g6lc_ooo_dispatch` previously blocked cancelled PRF writes but still let their
writeback-valid pulses clear rename busy state, wake IQ consumers, and override
operands through bypass. A mispredicted producer can release its physical register
before its late response arrives; another live producer can own that register.
